##### Output-Gate Rank Allocation.

Doubling the output-gate rank from r_{g}=128 to 256 requires R_{\mathrm{tot}}=640 for no or static {\bm{\gamma}} and R_{\mathrm{tot}}=768 for dynamic {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}, which shrinks d_{\mathrm{ffn}} by 128 and 256 channels, respectively. While (64,64,256,256,128) achieves slightly lower validation NLL at 166\text{M} and 834\text{M}, Muir surpasses it at 406\text{M} and outperforms all three r_{g}=256 variants at 1.34\text{B} across validation NLL, zero-shot commonsense reasoning, Based recall, and 5-shot TriviaQA. At larger scale, preserving d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=4d-512 and two-tile R_{\mathrm{tot}}=512 alignment outweighs a wider output gate.

##### Sensitivity-Allocated vs. Uniform Gate Ranks.

When readout gain is omitted or static (r_{q}=0), four uniform rank-128 gates (128,128,128,128,0) sum to R_{\mathrm{tot}}=512 and perform comparably to the sensitivity-allocated (64,64,128,256,0) variants across scales. However, adding a fifth rank-128 gate for dynamic {\bm{\gamma}}_{t} under uniform allocation (128,128,128,128,128) raises the total rank to R_{\mathrm{tot}}=640, which breaks 256-column MXU tile alignment and narrows d_{\mathrm{ffn}} by 128 channels. Because state updates are much less sensitive to the decay and erase gates than to the write gate ([Propositions 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), [3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Remark 3.2 (Origin of the write-erase sensitivity asymmetry). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and[3.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Remark 3.3 (Regularization of oblique erase variance by low-rank projections). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), compressing (r_{f},r_{b}) to (64,64) in Muir fits the rank-128 dynamic readout gain within R_{\mathrm{tot}}=512 without reducing d_{\mathrm{ffn}}, and outperforms the uniform dynamic-{\bm{\gamma}}_{t} variant on validation NLL at every scale and on 1.34\text{B} zero-shot commonsense reasoning, Based recall, and 5-shot TriviaQA.

##### Decoupling Gate Compression from FFN Expansion.

To separate the effect of low-rank gating from FFN expansion under the 12d^{2} budget, we vary gate ranks and FFN width independently at 406\text{M} relative to GDN-2 (d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=4{,}352). Compressing the dense (b,w) gates into rank-128 projections alongside rank-128{\bm{\gamma}}_{t} (R_{\mathrm{tot}}=640) while holding the FFN fixed at d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=4{,}352 cuts gate-projection FLOPs by 2.8\times and reduces non-embedding parameters and FLOPs by 12.5\% (-38.9\text{M} parameters across all layers) at a validation NLL cost of only +7.4\text{ mnats}. Conversely, adding the same +38.9\text{M} parameters (+12.5\% non-embedding capacity, d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=5{,}504) to widen the GDN-2 FFN with dense gates improves validation NLL by -20.5\text{ mnats} (2.8\times larger NLL reduction per parameter). Reinvesting the freed parameters into d_{\mathrm{ffn}} and low-rank {\bm{\gamma}}_{t} in iso-parameter Muir achieves -18.0\text{ mnats}, recovering 88\% of the unconstrained wide-FFN gain at zero parameter or FLOP overhead. By contrast, parameterizing {\bm{\gamma}}_{t} with a dense d\times d projection in GDN-2 forces d_{\mathrm{ffn}} down to 3{,}584 under the iso-parameter budget and degrades validation NLL by +8.3\text{ mnats}, which confirms that under an iso-parameter budget, dynamic readout requires a low-rank rather than dense parameterization.

## 6 Mechanistic Analysis

We now evaluate our theoretical predictions from §[3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.SS1 "3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") directly on the 100\text{B}-token checkpoints without retraining (protocols and supplementary tables are in [Appendix I](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9 "Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

![Image 10: Refer to caption](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376v1/figs/fig10_gate_sensitivity.png)

Figure 10: Trained models exhibit the predicted write-erase sensitivity asymmetry under both SVD rank truncation and causal gate freezing. (a) Truncating each rank-128 gate projection of the uniform-rank Muir checkpoint via SVD (one gate at a time across all layers) shows that the decay (f) and erase (b) gates saturate by rank 64, whereas the output (g), write (w), and readout gain (q) gates require rank 128. (b) Replacing the dynamic write gate with its token-averaged channel mean ({\bm{w}}_{t}\leftarrow\bar{\bm{w}}) across all layers degrades validation NLL 14.5\times more than freezing the erase gate ({\bm{b}}_{t}\leftarrow\bar{\bm{b}}) in both GDN-2 and Muir.

![Image 11: Refer to caption](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376v1/figs/fig11_row_energy.png)

Figure 11: Stationary state row energies obey the closed-form law and are balanced by fractional power-law readout gains. (a) Across state channels, the analytical fixed point {m}_{j}^{\ast} ([5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) closely tracks measured stationary row energy {m}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}\|_{2}^{2}] in both GDN-2 and Muir. (b) Across all heads, weighting state rows by the learned readout gain compresses the within-head \mathrm{p90}/\mathrm{p10} energy spread by 35\% relative to GDN-2. (c) Within each head, learned static gains |{\gamma}_{j}| follow a fractional power law {\gamma}_{j}\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-p} (p=0.35) of stationary row RMS {\rho}_{j}, intermediate between unit readout (p=0) and full whitening (p=1).

##### Write-Erase Gate Sensitivity.

[Figure 10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F10 "In 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") confirms the write-erase sensitivity asymmetry of [Proposition 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") in trained models and explains why compressing (f,b) to rank 64 while keeping (g,w,q) at rank 128 in ([9](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E9 "Equation 9 ‣ 4.1 Low-Rank Gating and Matched-Filter Readout ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) preserves model quality. In Figure [10 a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F10 "Figure 10 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), truncating the singular values of f or b from rank 128 to rank 64 across all layers of the 406\text{M} uniform-rank Muir checkpoint increases validation NLL by at most 0.6\text{ mnats}, whereas truncating q,w, or g to rank 64 increases validation NLL by 4.5,6.4, and 247\text{ mnats}, respectively. Figure [10 b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F10 "Figure 10 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") tests this asymmetry directly on the recurrence at 834\text{M}: freezing {\bm{w}}_{t} degrades validation NLL across all three position buckets and increases overall NLL 14.5\times more than freezing {\bm{b}}_{t} in both GDN-2 (+0.833 vs. +0.0574\text{ nats}) and Muir (+0.385 vs. +0.0266\text{ nats}). Consistent with [Remark 3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Remark 3.2 (Origin of the write-erase sensitivity asymmetry). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), erase perturbations have limited effect on the state because post-convolution \mathrm{SiLU} keys concentrate their energy onto a small subspace, with a key participation ratio of only 7.43 in GDN-2 and 5.98 in Muir out of d_{k}=128 channels ([Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.T3 "In Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

##### Stationary Row-Energy Scaling.

[Figure 11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F11 "In 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") evaluates the stationary row-energy law ([Proposition 3.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) and learned readout scaling ([Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). In Figure [11 a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F11 "Figure 11 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), the closed-form prediction {m}_{j}^{\ast} in ([5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) matches the empirical row energy {m}_{j} across state channels at 166\text{M} with Spearman correlation r_{s}=0.957 in GDN-2 and 0.960 in Muir (static {\bm{\gamma}}). Since the readout {\bm{o}}_{t}=({\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t}){\bm{S}}_{t} scales each state row j by {\gamma}_{t,j}, weighting {m}_{j} by the mean readout gain \bar{\gamma}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[{\gamma}_{t,j}] in Muir reduces the median within-head \mathrm{p90}/\mathrm{p10} spread of gain-weighted row energy \bar{\gamma}_{j}^{2}{m}_{j} from 14.7\times in GDN-2 to 9.6\times (Figure [11 b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F11 "Figure 11 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), [Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.T3 "In Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Consistent with [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), regressing \log_{10}|{\gamma}_{j}| against \log_{10}{\rho}_{j} within each head (Figure [11 c](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F11 "Figure 11 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) yields a median log-log slope of -0.35, which corresponds to a fractional power-law exponent p=0.35\in(0,1) in {\gamma}_{j}\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-p}.

![Image 12: Refer to caption](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376v1/figs/fig12_gamma_counterfactual.png)

Figure 12: Readout-exponent sweeps and erase-logit shifts confirm the optimality of fractional power-law readout and the early-vs.-late erasure trade-off. (a) Replacing the learned static gain in each head with an RMS-matched power law {\gamma}_{j}(p)\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-p} yields a U-shaped loss curve minimized near the learned exponent (p\approx 0.35), outperforming both unit readout (p=0) and full whitening (p=1). (b) Shifting the pre-activation erase logit ({\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}\leftarrow{\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}+\delta_{b}) shows that weaker erasure degrades predictions across all positions, whereas stronger erasure leaves early tokens [0,16) nearly unchanged while degrading late tokens [256,4\text{K}).

Adjacent self-erasure\mathbb{E}[|\langle{\bm{k}}_{t-1},{\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t}\rangle|]1.34B 0.406 0.328-19.2%[Remark 3.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem11 "Remark 3.11 (Mechanism of memory-horizon extension). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")

##### Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions.

In Figure [12 a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F12 "Figure 12 ‣ Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), replacing the learned static gain at 166\text{M} with a pure power law of row RMS, {\gamma}_{j}(p)\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-p}, increases validation NLL by +0.10\text{ nats} at p=0 (where slow rows dominate readout) and +0.31\text{ nats} at p=1 (where noisy fast rows are over-amplified; [Remark 3.9](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem9 "Remark 3.9 (Suboptimality of full inverse-RMS whitening). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), but comes within +0.037\text{ nats} of the unconstrained learned gain at p=0.25 (+0.063\text{ nats} at p=0.50) near the fitted exponent p=0.35. In Figure [12 b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F12 "Figure 12 ‣ Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), shifting the erase logit {\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}\leftarrow{\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}+\delta_{b} at 834\text{M} confirms the horizon trade-off of [Proposition 3.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem10 "Proposition 3.10 (Erase-readout substitution). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"): weakening erasure (\delta_{b}=-1.0) increases interference across all positions (+0.031\text{--}0.044\text{ nats} at [256,4\text{K})), whereas strengthening erasure (\delta_{b}=+0.5) leaves early tokens [0,16) within noise (-0.003\pm 0.005\text{ nats} in Muir) while degrading late tokens [256,4\text{K}) (+0.008\text{--}0.015\text{ nats}) by overwriting stored context. Consistent with [Proposition 3.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem10 "Proposition 3.10 (Erase-readout substitution). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and [Remark 3.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem11 "Remark 3.11 (Mechanism of memory-horizon extension). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), suppressing slow-row interference at readout lets 1.34\text{B} Muir ([Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.T3 "In Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) operate with a 16.6\% lower parallel erase rate \bar{\mu} (0.652 vs. 0.782) and 19.2\% lower adjacent self-erasure (0.328 vs. 0.406), preserving +16.1\% higher state spectral effective rank and a 59.6\% longer temporal lookback window (49.3\to 78.7 tokens; [Section I.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.SS3 "I.3 Counterfactual Interventions and Memory-Horizon Diagnostics ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

![Image 13: Refer to caption](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376v1/figs/fig13_mv_hop_recall.png)

Figure 13: Muir preserves early variable bindings and routes readout to the target hop at each answer step. (a) On 4-value MV-NIAH, Transformer drops on the final write V_{4} and unit-readout recurrences degrade after V_{1}, whereas Muir remains accurate across V_{1}\text{--}V_{4}. (b) On 5-hop RULER VT, Transformer recalls only the root assignment H_{1}, whereas Muir maintains accuracy across all five hops H_{1}\text{--}H_{5}. (c) Within-model root-needle (H_{1}) retention in {\bm{S}}_{t} from write milestone s_{1} (100\%) to query milestone t_{\mathrm{q}} across 16 held-out prompts (interquartile bands across prompts). (d) Within-model retention of written needles H_{1}\text{--}H_{5} from each write (100\%) across sequence milestones s_{1}\text{--}s_{5} to t_{\mathrm{q}}. (e) Share of readout weight \hat{\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t} (over the haystack) on each needle H_{1}\text{--}H_{5} across the four answer-generation steps A_{1}\text{--}A_{4} that chain H_{1}\to\dotsb\to H_{5}.

##### Multi-Value Recall and Multi-Hop Variable Tracking.

[Figure 13](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "In Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") connects our state-space diagnostics ([Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.T3 "In Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) to the 4-value MV-NIAH and 5-hop VT gains in §[5.2.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.SS2.SSS2 "5.2.2 Associative Recall ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") (details in [Section I.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.SS4 "I.4 Multi-Value Recall and Multi-Hop Variable Tracking ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). On MV-NIAH, Transformer recalls V_{1}\text{--}V_{3}, but query heads attend to already-emitted answer tokens on the fourth value V_{4} (Figure [13 a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Consistent with latent multi-hop limits ([Biran et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib16); [Yang et al., 2025a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib122)), Transformer resolves H_{1} on VT but fails to chain H_{2}\text{--}H_{5} in one pass (Figure [13 b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Recurrent models propagate bindings sequentially through {\bm{S}}_{t}, but repeated writes to the same state rows compete for memory. In GDN-2, heavy write-time erasure (\bar{\mu}=0.782) prematurely overwrites early bindings (V_{1}\text{--}V_{3} and H_{1}\text{--}H_{3}). Removing {\bm{\gamma}}_{t} in Muir lets V_{1} accumulate in slow rows that mask V_{2}\text{--}V_{4} ([Proposition 3.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Static {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} equalizes write order on MV-NIAH ([Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), but one fixed gain cannot match five hop distances ([Remark 3.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem7 "Remark 3.7 (Incompatibility of static gain across retrieval lags). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Decomposing {\bm{o}}_{t}=\hat{\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t} into per-needle write contributions, we track within-model retention of each hop write (s_{1}\text{--}s_{5}) in {\bm{S}}_{t} until query token t_{\mathrm{q}} (Figure [13 c,d](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) and how much weight \hat{\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t} places on each hop across answer steps A_{1}\text{--}A_{4} (Figure [13 e](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Lower write-time erasure ([Proposition 3.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem10 "Proposition 3.10 (Erase-readout substitution). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) raises retention in Muir at t_{\mathrm{q}} over GDN-2 from 37\% to 45\% on H_{1} and from 46\% to 52\% on average across H_{1}\text{--}H_{5} (Figure [13 c,d](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Across A_{1}\text{--}A_{4}, dynamic {\bm{\gamma}}_{t} shifts the channel filter ([Proposition 3.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) so \hat{\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t} concentrates weight along the target diagonal H_{1}\text{--}H_{4}, most notably on the oldest root hop (18\% to 23\% at A_{1}; Figure [13 e](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

## 7 Conclusion

Channel-wise gates give delta-rule models fine-grained control over a fixed-size state, but its effective capacity depends on the state dynamics those gates induce. In our trained models, key activation and unit normalization concentrate key energy onto roughly 5\% of channels per head, making the state 13\times more sensitive to write than to erase perturbations; Muir therefore fuses all gates into a low-rank projection with smaller decay and erase bottlenecks, widening the FFN by up to 30.4\% at fixed parameters and FLOPs. Meanwhile, consistent with our theory, multi-timescale decay drives state row-energy variation across orders of magnitude, exceeding 100\times in heads mixing fast and slow channels. Compared to GDN-2, Muir reduces median readout-energy spread by 35\% and write-time erasure by 17\%, improves root-needle multi-hop retention from 37\% to 45\%, and lowers the median condition number of the chunkwise triangular solve by 24\%.

Our results also reveal two remaining limitations. First, under a fixed gate-rank budget, static and dynamic readout trade single-horizon retention against multi-lag adaptability. On passkey lookup, a static filter suffices for a single query lag and preserves write rank for near-perfect recall to 1\text{M} tokens, whereas halving write rank to fund dynamic readout limits single-needle retention beyond 32\text{K}. Conversely, static readout cannot match varying lags on multi-hop and extractive tasks, and drifts on continuous text as slow-row energy accumulates, whereas dynamic readout adapts per token to resolve multi-lag queries and hold flat perplexity across 10\text{M}-token streams. Second, while our pure-JAX kernel matches GDN-2 throughput and trains 2.74\times faster than Transformer at 32\text{K}, Splash Attention remains faster at short contexts (<\!11\text{K}) because its Pallas kernel fuses masking and block contractions in VMEM while XLA materializes chunk temporaries in HBM.

These limitations suggest several directions for future work. Parameterizing dynamic readout through our analytical matched filter by predicting one lag per head and tracking expected row energy could unify distant passkey recall, multi-lag routing, and stream stability without sacrificing write rank. Alternatively, pairing _sliding-window_ attention with static-readout Muir would resolve this tension architecturally: local attention copies nearby spans exactly in constant memory, freeing the recurrent state to keep full write rank for distant lookup without periodic global attention or a growing KV cache. At the kernel level, fusing leaf Neumann inversion, the boundary-pivoted merge tree, and the cross-chunk state scan into a single Pallas kernel would keep chunk temporaries in VMEM and narrow the short-context throughput gap. Beyond gated delta recurrences, sensitivity-allocated gating and matched-filter readout extend to matrix-state test-time optimization models whose channels decay or update at different rates under unweighted queries.
