## Appendix

## Appendix A Additional Related Work

##### Linear Attention, State-Space Models, and Fast-Weight Memories.

Replacing the {\mathcal{O}}(T) key-value cache and {\mathcal{O}}(T^{2}) attention matrix of softmax attention [[Vaswani et al., 2017](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib116)] with a fixed-size associative state matrix has roots in Hopfield networks [[Hopfield, 1982](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib48)] and fast-weight controllers [[Schmidhuber, 1992](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib102), [Ba et al., 2016](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib8)]. Kernelized linear transformers [[Katharopoulos et al., 2020](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib60), [Choromanski et al., 2020](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib23), [Pham and Pagh, 2013](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib87), [Kacham et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib58), [Ghriss and Chakraborty, 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib37)] factorize the attention kernel into feature maps so that causal sequence mixing reduces to an additive outer-product recurrence {\bm{S}}_{t}={\bm{S}}_{t-1}+{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{t}, but purely additive accumulation suffers from unbounded state growth and associative cross-talk once sequence length exceeds the key dimension [[Schlag et al., 2021](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib101), [Arora et al., 2023a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib3), [Jelassi et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib53), [Arora et al., 2024a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib5), [Arora et al., 2024b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib6)]. Structured state-space models (SSMs), long-convolution architectures, and fixed-decay linear recurrences such as S4 [[Gu et al., 2021](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib40)], BiGS [[Wang et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib118)], H3 [[Fu et al., 2022](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib33)], Hyena [[Poli et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib88)], LRU [[Orvieto et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib80)], RWKV [[Peng et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib83)], and RetNet [[Sun et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib113)] introduce data-independent exponential decay across state channels or heads, while selective SSMs and gated linear recurrences, including Mamba [[Gu and Dao, 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib39)], Mamba-2 [[Dao and Gu, 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib27)], Gated Attention Unit (FLASH; [Hua et al., 2022](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib51)), Griffin [[De et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib29)], HGRN [[Qin et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib89)], HGRN2 [[Qin et al., 2024b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib91)], xLSTM [[Beck et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib10)], Gated Linear Attention (GLA; [Yang et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib124)), Gated Slot Attention (GSA; [Zhang et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib130)), ReGLA [[Lu et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib70)], and Mamba-3 [[Lahoti et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib63)], make decay gates data-dependent so the model can selectively forget stale context [[van der Westhuizen and Lasenby, 2018](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib115), [Sun et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib111)]. Although data-dependent exponential decay bounds the total state norm, diagonal decay scales an entire state channel uniformly and cannot selectively erase a single key-value association without flushing other associations stored along the same channel [[Yang et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib125), [Yang et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126)].

##### Delta-Rule Recurrences and Online Test-Time Optimization.

To support targeted associative updates, Fast Weight Programmers [[Schlag et al., 2021](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib101)] and DeltaNet [[Yang et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib125)] replace additive writes with the Widrow-Hoff delta rule [[Widrow and Hoff, 1960](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib120)], which subtracts the current state prediction {\bm{k}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t-1} along key {\bm{k}}_{t} prior to writing the new value. Gated DeltaNet [[Yang et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126)] combines scalar exponential decay with a scalar Householder-like delta transition; more broadly, selective SSMs and gated linear recurrences are widely interleaved with softmax attention in hybrid sequence models [[Ren et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib96), [Dong et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib31), [Lenz et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib65), [MiniMax et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib76), [Kimi Team et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib61), [Qiu et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib93)]. Subsequent extensions introduce vector or matrix-valued erasure and decay in RWKV-7 [[Peng et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib84)], Kimi Linear (KDA; [Kimi Team et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib61)), and GDN-2 [[Hatamizadeh et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42)], cross-layer value and residual routing in [Cerruti et al. [2026]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib22), multi-step or negative-eigenvalue state transitions in [Grazzi et al. [2024]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib38), DeltaProduct [[Siems et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib107)], and Comba [[Hu et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib50)], non-linear matrix-state transitions in \mathrm{M}^{2}RNN [[Mishra et al., 2026b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib78)], sparse slot-addressed state expansion in Sparse Delta Memory [[Cabannes et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib21)], and hierarchical or higher-order recurrent states in Log-Linear Attention [[Guo et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib41)] and Triadic Linear Attention [[Sieberling et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib106)]. A parallel line of work interprets these recurrences as online test-time optimization of an internal regression or associative memory objective: Test-Time Training (TTT; [Sun et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib112), [Zhang et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib129), [Liu et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib67)), Longhorn [[Liu et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib66)], Titans [[Behrouz et al., 2025c](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib14)], ATLAS [[Behrouz et al., 2025a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib12)], Miras [[Behrouz et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib13)], Test-Time Regression [[Wang et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib119)], MesaNet [[von Oswald et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib117)], Lattice [[Karami et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib59)], Gated KalmaNet [[Peng et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib86)], Kaczmarz Linear Attention [[Zou et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib131)], and Momentum Delta Networks [[Huang et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib52)] derive recurrent state updates as single-step gradient, momentum, Kaczmarz, Newton, or Kalman updates on a key-value reconstruction loss (see [Appendix C](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A3 "Appendix C Online Regression Formulation ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") for the optimization lens). Across these architectures, KDA [[Kimi Team et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib61)] and RWKV-7 [[Peng et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib84)] retain a 4d FFN by avoiding dense d\times d recurrence gates: KDA restricts key erasure and value writes to a shared per-head scalar alongside low-rank decay and output gates, while RWKV-7 uses low-rank decay, removal, value-residual, and output gates with decoupled removal and replacement keys. By contrast, GDN-2 [[Hatamizadeh et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42)] decouples channel-wise erasure and writes on a shared key via dense d\times d projections at the expense of FFN width [[Shazeer, 2020](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib105), [Geva et al., 2021](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib36)]. For state readout, whereas query-key normalized attention [[Hua et al., 2022](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib51), [Dehghani et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib30)] and diagonal SSMs [[Lahoti et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib63)] apply learned per-channel gains to both queries and keys after normalization, delta-rule recurrences require unit-norm keys (\|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{2}=1) to keep rank-one erasure contractive and therefore evaluate the unweighted product {\bm{o}}_{t}={\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t} (or a scalar per-head rescaling; [Hu et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib50)) after parameter-free \ell_{2} normalization [[Henry et al., 2020](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib47), [Schlag et al., 2021](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib101), [Yang et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib125)] and post-readout gating [[Qiu et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib92)]. Recent test-time optimization models instead precondition queries via a running key-covariance state [[von Oswald et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib117), [Peng et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib86), [Le et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib64)] or orthogonalize {\bm{S}}_{t} via Newton-Schulz iterations during training [[Aquino-Michaels, 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib2)]. Our analysis in §[3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3 "3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") shows that state updates are much more sensitive to write-gate than to erase-gate perturbations ([Proposition 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), allowing all five gates to share a low-rank bottleneck, and that a diagonal matched-filter readout {\bm{o}}_{t}=({\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t}){\bm{S}}_{t} corrects the row-energy imbalance of channel-wise decay ([Propositions 3.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), [3.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and[3.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem10 "Proposition 3.10 (Erase-readout substitution). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) without covariance state or matrix orthogonalization.

##### Hardware-Efficient Chunkwise Algorithms.

Executing token-level matrix recurrences efficiently on modern accelerators requires reformulating sequential outer products into block-parallel matrix multiplications [[Blelloch, 1989](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib19), [Dao et al., 2022](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib28), [Dao, 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib26), [Shah et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib104)]. For diagonal decay recurrences, FLASH [[Hua et al., 2022](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib51)], RetNet [[Sun et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib113)], GLA [[Yang et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib124)], Lightning Attention-2 [[Qin et al., 2024a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib90)], Mamba-2 [[Dao and Gu, 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib27), [Santoni, 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib99)], FLA [[Yang and Zhang, 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib123)], and Tiled Flash Linear Attention [[Beck et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib11)] partition the sequence into chunks of length C, compute within-chunk outputs via masked matrix-matrix multiplications, and propagate the state across chunk boundaries. When the state transition includes rank-one key erasure \mathbf{I}-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}, unrolling a chunk of C transitions yields a product of C rank-one perturbations. DeltaNet [[Yang et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib125)] and Gated DeltaNet [[Yang et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126)] compact this product using the classical WY representation of Householder transformations [[Bischof and Van Loan, 1987](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib17), [Schreiber and Van Loan, 1989](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib103), [Joffrain et al., 2006](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib55)], which reduces within-chunk parallelization to solving a C\times C unit lower-triangular system {\bm{T}}=\mathbf{I}+{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{strict}}. On GPUs, FLA [[Yang and Zhang, 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib123)] and KDA [[Kimi Team et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib61)] subdivide each C=64 chunk into 16-token sub-chunks [[Yang et al., 2023](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib124)], compute decays relative to sub-chunk boundaries instead of dividing by full-chunk prefixes, invert the four diagonal 16\times 16 blocks sequentially in SRAM, and merge off-diagonal 16\times 16 blocks on TensorCores. On TPUs, however, the matrix multiply unit (MXU) is a 256\times 256 systolic array while the vector processing unit (VPU) is over 60\times slower in bfloat16[[Austin et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib7), [Jiang et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib54)], so 16\times 16 tiles under-utilize the MXU and sequential leaf substitution stalls on the VPU. Concurrent work on FLAT [[Mishra et al., 2026a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib77)] provides hand-tuned Pallas TPU kernels for diagonal-decay recurrences (Linear Attention, Mamba-2, GLA) without the delta-rule triangular solve; FlyWheel [[Bao et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib9)] develops Pallas TPU kernels for softmax attention and for Gated DeltaNet inference via MXU triangular inversion; and [Sobczyk et al. [2026]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib109) benchmark matrix-product-rich triangular inversion (including hybrid leaf-Neumann and recursive block-merge forward kernels) on an already-materialized {\bm{T}} on GPUs and NPUs. In §[4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.SS2 "4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), rather than materializing {\bm{T}} upfront via prefix division, we form cross-blocks via boundary-pivoted log-decay splitting at each merge step of recursive block-Neumann inversion [[Heller, 1974](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib44), [Orcutt, 1974](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib79), [Heller, 1978](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib45), [Bini and Pan, 1994](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib15), [Sobczyk et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib109)] and differentiate the solve in two full-chunk GEMMs with on-the-fly VMEM pivot recomputation, routing 98\% of C=256 training FLOPs onto 256\times 256 MXU contractions.

## Appendix B Summary of Notation

Table 4: Summary of notation. All vectors are row vectors; hats (\hat{\cdot}) denote gate-modulated vectors or decayed states, bars (\bar{\cdot}) denote expectations or empirical means, and asterisks (∗) denote stationary fixed points or optimal solutions.

## Appendix C Online Regression Formulation

Here, we show how the state update and readout of GDN-2 and Muir arise from a preconditioned online regression objective [[Schlag et al., 2021](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib101), [Yang et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib125), [Sun et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib112), [Liu et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib66), [Yang et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126), [Behrouz et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib13), [Wang et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib119), [Hatamizadeh et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42)], and summarize how Muir compares with prior linear-time sequence models ([Table 5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A3.T5 "In Matched-Filter Readout. ‣ Appendix C Online Regression Formulation ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

##### Online Associative Memory Update.

Consider a single head maintaining a linear associative memory {\bm{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{v}} that maps keys {\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d_{k}-1} to values {\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times{d_{v}}} via {\bm{k}}\mapsto{\bm{k}}{\bm{S}}. At timestep t, updating {\bm{S}}_{t-1}\to{\bm{S}}_{t} upon observing ({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{v}}_{t}) proceeds in two stages. First, before incorporating the new observation, the prior state {\bm{S}}_{t-1} is contracted row-wise via the channel-wise decay operator \hat{\bm{S}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Diag({\bm{\alpha}}_{t}){\bm{S}}_{t-1}, which minimizes the row-weighted Frobenius proximity objective \|{\bm{S}}-{\bm{S}}_{t-1}\|_{F}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}({\alpha}_{t,j}^{-1}-1)\|{\bm{S}}_{j,:}\|_{2}^{2}. Second, given the decayed state \hat{\bm{S}}_{t}, consider the gated key-value reconstruction loss

{\mathcal{L}}_{t}({\bm{S}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}{\bm{S}}-\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\text{where}\qquad\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t},\quad\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\bm{w}}_{t}\odot{\bm{v}}_{t},

whose Euclidean gradient with respect to {\bm{S}} is \nabla_{{\bm{S}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{t}({\bm{S}})=\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}{\bm{S}}-\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}). Applying a left row preconditioner {\bm{P}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Diag({\bm{b}}_{t})^{-1} at {\bm{S}}=\hat{\bm{S}}_{t} so that the update direction aligns with the unit key {\bm{k}}_{t}=\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}\Diag({\bm{b}}_{t})^{-1} yields the single-step update

{\bm{S}}_{t}=\hat{\bm{S}}_{t}-{\bm{P}}_{t}\nabla_{{\bm{S}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(\hat{\bm{S}}_{t})=\hat{\bm{S}}_{t}-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}\big(\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}\hat{\bm{S}}_{t}-\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}\big)=\big(\mathbf{I}-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}({\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t})\big)\Diag({\bm{\alpha}}_{t}){\bm{S}}_{t-1}+{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}({\bm{w}}_{t}\odot{\bm{v}}_{t}),

which recovers the state update in GDN-2 ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S2.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ Gated DeltaNet-2. ‣ 2.1 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Rule ‣ 2 Preliminaries ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) and Muir ([8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E8 "Equation 8 ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

##### Matched-Filter Readout.

In standard delta-rule architectures (DeltaNet, Gated DeltaNet, RWKV-7, KDA, GDN-2), querying {\bm{S}}_{t} with {\bm{q}}_{t} evaluates the unweighted readout {\bm{o}}_{t}={\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{q}_{t,j}{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}. However, because each row j\in\{1,\dotsc,d_{k}\} evolves under its own decay factor {\alpha}_{t,j} and erase gate {b}_{t,j}, the rows of {\bm{S}}_{t} accumulate multi-write interference with unequal stationary variance {m}_{j}^{\ast}=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}\|_{2}^{2}] ([Proposition 3.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). When querying a target association written \ell timesteps earlier, the j-th row carries signal amplitude proportional to {\lambda}_{j}^{\ell\mkern-0.5mu\smash{\raisebox{0.55pt}{\scalebox{0.78}{$\scriptstyle/$}}}\mkern-0.5mu2} and noise variance {m}_{j}^{\ast}. By [Proposition 3.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), the minimum-MSE linear estimator weights each row j by its signal-to-noise ratio {\gamma}_{t,j}\propto\bar{a}_{\ell,j}/{m}_{j}^{\ast}\approx{\lambda}_{j}^{\ell\mkern-0.5mu\smash{\raisebox{0.55pt}{\scalebox{0.78}{$\scriptstyle/$}}}\mkern-0.5mu2}/{m}_{j}^{\ast}, which is equivalent to querying {\bm{S}}_{t} with the gain-modulated query \hat{\bm{q}}_{t}={\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t} in ([8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E8 "Equation 8 ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")):

{\bm{o}}_{t}=\hat{\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t}=\big({\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t}\big){\bm{S}}_{t}={\bm{q}}_{t}\Diag({\bm{\gamma}}_{t}){\bm{S}}_{t}.

As summarized in [Table 5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A3.T5 "In Matched-Filter Readout. ‣ Appendix C Online Regression Formulation ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), prior delta-rule models restrict state decay and key erasure to per-head scalars (DeltaNet, Gated DeltaNet), pair channel-wise decay with scalar erasure (KDA), use low-rank decay and removal gates on decoupled removal and replacement keys (RWKV-7), or use dense d\times d projections for channel-wise erasure and write gating (GDN-2), all with unweighted readout {\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t}. By contrast, Muir pairs channel-wise decay, erasure, and write gating on a shared key with gain-modulated readout ({\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t}){\bm{S}}_{t} while parameterizing all five channel-wise gates via a fused low-rank projection.

Table 5: Comparison of per-head state recurrence, query readout, and gate projection parameterizations across linear-time sequence models. For each model, we list the per-head decay, key-erasure, and value-write factors, the query readout, and whether each learned gate projection across heads is a per-head scalar (d\to N_{h}), low-rank bottleneck (d\to r\to d), or dense matrix (d\to d). (†RetNet fixes its per-head decay scalar {\alpha} without an input projection; ‡RWKV-7 combines decay and removal additively, writes with a decoupled replacement key \tilde{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{t}, and includes a low-rank cross-layer value-residual gate.)

## Appendix D Mathematical Proofs

### D.1 First-Order Gate Sensitivity and Spectral Norm of Oblique Erase Transitions

###### Proof of [Proposition 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

(i) Because \hat{\bm{S}}_{t} does not depend on ({\bm{b}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{t}), the update ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S2.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ Gated DeltaNet-2. ‣ 2.1 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Rule ‣ 2 Preliminaries ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) is affine in {\bm{b}}_{t} and in {\bm{w}}_{t} separately. For any {\epsilon}\neq 0,{\epsilon}^{-1}({\bm{S}}_{t}({\bm{b}}_{t}+{\epsilon}\delta{\bm{b}},{\bm{w}}_{t})-{\bm{S}}_{t}({\bm{b}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{t}))=-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}\big((\delta{\bm{b}}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t})\hat{\bm{S}}_{t}\big) and {\epsilon}^{-1}({\bm{S}}_{t}({\bm{b}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{t}+{\epsilon}\delta{\bm{w}})-{\bm{S}}_{t}({\bm{b}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{t}))={\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}(\delta{\bm{w}}\odot{\bm{v}}_{t}), which gives ([2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E2 "Equation 2 ‣ Item 0(i) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

(ii) For any {\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times{d_{k}}} and {\bm{r}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times{d_{v}}}, the rank-one outer product {\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{r}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{v}} satisfies \|{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{r}}\|_{F}^{2}=\Tr({\bm{r}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{r}})=\|{\bm{u}}\|_{2}^{2}\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}. Applying this to ([2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E2 "Equation 2 ‣ Item 0(i) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) with {\bm{u}}={\bm{k}}_{t} and \|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=1 gives \|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{b}}}{\bm{S}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{b}}]\|_{F}^{2}=\|{\bm{r}}_{b}\|_{2}^{2} and \|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{w}}}{\bm{S}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{w}}]\|_{F}^{2}=\|{\bm{r}}_{w}\|_{2}^{2}, where {\bm{r}}_{b}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\delta{\bm{b}}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t})\hat{\bm{S}}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\delta{b}_{j}{k}_{t,j}\hat{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:} and {\bm{r}}_{w}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\delta{\bm{w}}\odot{\bm{v}}_{t}. Taking expectations over \delta{\bm{b}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d_{k}}) and \delta{\bm{w}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d_{v}}) using \mathbb{E}[\delta{b}_{i}\delta{b}_{j}]=\delta_{ij} and \mathbb{E}[\delta{w}_{j}^{2}]=1 yields ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ Item 0(ii) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")):

\mathbb{E}_{\delta{\bm{b}}}\!\left[\|{\bm{r}}_{b}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\delta{b}_{i}\delta{b}_{j}]\,{k}_{t,i}{k}_{t,j}\langle\hat{\bm{S}}_{t,i,:},\hat{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,j}^{2}\|\hat{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\mathbb{E}_{\delta{\bm{w}}}\!\left[\|{\bm{r}}_{w}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{v}}\mathbb{E}[\delta{w}_{j}^{2}]\,{v}_{t,j}^{2}=\|{\bm{v}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.

(iii) Since \langle{\bm{s}}_{t},{\bm{k}}_{t}\rangle=0,\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,j}^{2}=1, and \sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,j}^{2}{b}_{t,j}={\mu}_{t}, the Pythagorean theorem gives

\|{\bm{s}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-{\mu}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,j}^{2}{b}_{t,j}^{2}-{\mu}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,j}^{2}\big({b}_{t,j}-{\mu}_{t}\big)^{2}.

Differentiating {\mu}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{b}_{t,j}{k}_{t,j}^{2} with respect to {\bm{b}}_{t} gives \nabla_{{\bm{b}}}{\mu}_{t}={\bm{k}}_{t}^{\odot 2}, so \|\nabla_{{\bm{b}}}{\mu}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,j}^{4}=\|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{4}^{4}=\eta_{\mathrm{eff}}({\bm{k}}_{t})^{-1}. Since each {k}_{t,j}^{2}\in[0,1], we have {k}_{t,j}^{4}\leq{k}_{t,j}^{2} and summing over j gives \|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{4}^{4}\leq 1 (with equality if and only if {\bm{k}}_{t} is 1-sparse). Conversely, Cauchy-Schwarz applied to {\bm{k}}_{t}^{\odot 2} and {\bm{1}}_{d_{k}} yields 1=\langle{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\odot 2},{\bm{1}}_{d_{k}}\rangle^{2}\leq d_{k}\|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{4}^{4} (with equality if and only if |{k}_{t,j}|=d_{k}^{-1\mkern-0.5mu\smash{\raisebox{0.55pt}{\scalebox{0.78}{$\scriptstyle/$}}}\mkern-0.5mu2} for all j), which completes the proof. ∎

In Proposition [0(iii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.I1.i3 "Item 0(iii) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and [Remark 3.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Remark 3.3 (Regularization of oblique erase variance by low-rank projections). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), we decompose the erase-gated key \hat{\bm{k}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times{d_{k}}} relative to the unit key {\bm{k}}_{t}\in\mathbb{S}^{d_{k}-1} (d_{k}\geq 2) into its parallel component {\mu}_{t}{\bm{k}}_{t} and orthogonal residual {\bm{s}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}-{\mu}_{t}{\bm{k}}_{t} as

\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}={\mu}_{t}{\bm{k}}_{t}+{\bm{s}}_{t}={\mu}_{t}{\bm{k}}_{t}+{s}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t},\qquad{\mu}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t}\rangle\in(0,2),\qquad{s}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\|{\bm{s}}_{t}\|_{2}\geq 0,

where \langle{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{u}}_{t}\rangle=0 and \|{\bm{u}}_{t}\|_{2}=1 (with {\bm{u}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\bm{s}}_{t}/{s}_{t} whenever {s}_{t}>0, and any unit vector orthogonal to {\bm{k}}_{t} when {s}_{t}=0). The following lemma establishes the exact spectral norm and lower bound of the rank-one erase transition matrix {\bm{E}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{I}-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\bm{k}}_{t} cited in [Remark 3.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Remark 3.3 (Regularization of oblique erase variance by low-rank projections). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

###### Lemma D.1(Spectral norm of oblique rank-one erase).

Let {\bm{k}}_{t}\in\mathbb{S}^{d_{k}-1} (d_{k}\geq 2) and {\bm{b}}_{t}\in(0,2)^{1\times d_{k}} with {\mu}_{t}\in(0,2),{s}_{t}=\|{\bm{s}}_{t}\|_{2}\geq 0, and \delta_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mu}_{t}(2-{\mu}_{t})\in(0,1]. Then the erase transition matrix {\bm{E}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{I}-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}({\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t}) satisfies

\|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}=\sqrt{1+\frac{{s}_{t}^{2}-\delta_{t}+\sqrt{({s}_{t}^{2}-\delta_{t})^{2}+4{s}_{t}^{2}}}{2}}\geq\sqrt{1+{s}_{t}^{2}},(13)

with the small-{s}_{t} expansion

\|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}=1+\frac{{s}_{t}^{2}}{2\delta_{t}}+{\mathcal{O}}({s}_{t}^{4})=1+\frac{\|{\bm{s}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2{\mu}_{t}(2-{\mu}_{t})}+{\mathcal{O}}(\|{\bm{s}}_{t}\|_{2}^{4}).

In particular, \|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}=1 if and only if {\bm{s}}_{t}={\bm{0}} (uniform erase {\bm{b}}_{t}={\mu}_{t}{\bm{1}} on the support of {\bm{k}}_{t}), and \|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{1+\|{\bm{s}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}>1 whenever {\bm{s}}_{t}\neq{\bm{0}}.

###### Proof.

For any vector {\bm{z}}\in\mathrm{span}({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{u}}_{t})^{\perp}, we have \langle{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{z}}\rangle=0 and \langle\hat{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{z}}\rangle=0, so {\bm{z}}{\bm{E}}_{t}={\bm{z}} and {\bm{E}}_{t}{\bm{z}}^{\top}={\bm{z}}^{\top}. Thus {\bm{E}}_{t} acts as the identity on the (d_{k}-2)-dimensional orthogonal complement of \mathrm{span}({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{u}}_{t}), with all (d_{k}-2) corresponding singular values equal to 1. On the two-dimensional invariant subspace \mathrm{span}({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{u}}_{t}) with orthonormal basis ({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{u}}_{t}), we have

{\bm{k}}_{t}{\bm{E}}_{t}={\bm{k}}_{t}-\underbrace{\langle{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{k}}_{t}\rangle}_{=1}\big({\mu}_{t}{\bm{k}}_{t}+{s}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t}\big)=(1-{\mu}_{t}){\bm{k}}_{t}-{s}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t},\qquad{\bm{u}}_{t}{\bm{E}}_{t}={\bm{u}}_{t}-\underbrace{\langle{\bm{u}}_{t},{\bm{k}}_{t}\rangle}_{=0}\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}={\bm{u}}_{t}.

Hence the restriction of {\bm{E}}_{t} to \mathrm{span}({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{u}}_{t}) has the 2\times 2 matrix representation and Gram matrix

{\bm{E}}_{t\mid 2}=\begin{bmatrix}1-{\mu}_{t}&-{s}_{t}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix},\qquad{\bm{G}}_{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\bm{E}}_{t\mid 2}{\bm{E}}_{t\mid 2}^{\top}=\begin{bmatrix}(1-{\mu}_{t})^{2}+{s}_{t}^{2}&-{s}_{t}\\
-{s}_{t}&1\end{bmatrix}.

Since (1-{\mu}_{t})^{2}=1-{\mu}_{t}(2-{\mu}_{t})=1-\delta_{t}, the trace and determinant of {\bm{G}}_{t} are

\Tr{\bm{G}}_{t}=2+{s}_{t}^{2}-\delta_{t},\qquad\det{\bm{G}}_{t}=(1-{\mu}_{t})^{2}=1-\delta_{t}.

Solving the characteristic polynomial \nu^{2}-(\Tr{\bm{G}}_{t})\nu+\det{\bm{G}}_{t}=0 for the larger eigenvalue \nu_{\max}({\bm{G}}_{t}) and taking its square root \|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\nu_{\max}({\bm{G}}_{t})} yields the exact equality in ([13](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A4.E13 "Equation 13 ‣ Lemma D.1 (Spectral norm of oblique rank-one erase). ‣ D.1 First-Order Gate Sensitivity and Spectral Norm of Oblique Erase Transitions ‣ Appendix D Mathematical Proofs ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Because \delta_{t}\in(0,1], we have \sqrt{({s}_{t}^{2}-\delta_{t})^{2}+4{s}_{t}^{2}}=\sqrt{({s}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})^{2}+4(1-\delta_{t}){s}_{t}^{2}}\geq{s}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t}, so \nu_{\max}({\bm{G}}_{t})\geq 1+{s}_{t}^{2}, which establishes the lower bound \|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{1+{s}_{t}^{2}}. Finally, expanding the inner square root as {s}_{t}\to 0 gives

\sqrt{(\delta_{t}-{s}_{t}^{2})^{2}+4{s}_{t}^{2}}=\sqrt{\delta_{t}^{2}+2(2-\delta_{t}){s}_{t}^{2}+{s}_{t}^{4}}=\delta_{t}-{s}_{t}^{2}+\frac{2{s}_{t}^{2}}{\delta_{t}}+{\mathcal{O}}({s}_{t}^{4}),

which implies \nu_{\max}({\bm{G}}_{t})=1+{s}_{t}^{2}/\delta_{t}+{\mathcal{O}}({s}_{t}^{4}) and \|{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2}=1+{s}_{t}^{2}/(2\delta_{t})+{\mathcal{O}}({s}_{t}^{4}). ∎

### D.2 Stationary Row-Energy Law and Fourth-Moment Expansion

###### Proof of [Proposition 3.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

From ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S2.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ Gated DeltaNet-2. ‣ 2.1 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Rule ‣ 2 Preliminaries ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), the j-th row of {\bm{S}}_{t}=\hat{\bm{S}}_{t}-{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}\hat{\bm{S}}_{t})+{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\bm{v}}_{t} decomposes as {\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}={\bm{a}}_{j}-{\bm{r}}_{j}+{\bm{w}}_{j}, where {\bm{a}}_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\alpha}_{t,j}{\bm{S}}_{t-1,j,:} is the decayed prior row, {\bm{w}}_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{k}_{t,j}\hat{\bm{v}}_{t} is the new write row, and {\bm{r}}_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{k}_{t,j}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}{b}_{t,i}{k}_{t,i}{\alpha}_{t,i}{\bm{S}}_{t-1,i,:} is the key-erasure row. (Note that if {\bm{S}}_{0}={\bm{0}} and key coordinates have zero odd cross-moments \mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}{k}_{t,j^{\prime}}{k}_{t,i}^{2}]=0 for j\neq j^{\prime}, taking the inner product \mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:},{\bm{S}}_{t,j^{\prime},:}\rangle] preserves row orthogonality \mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:},{\bm{S}}_{t,j^{\prime},:}\rangle]=\delta_{jj^{\prime}}{m}_{t,j} inductively for all t\geq 0.) Because \mathbb{E}[\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}]={\bm{0}} and \hat{\bm{v}}_{t} is independent of ({\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{\alpha}}_{t},{\bm{b}}_{t},{\bm{S}}_{t-1}), we have \mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{a}}_{j}-{\bm{r}}_{j},{\bm{w}}_{j}\rangle]=0, and expanding \|{\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}\|_{2}^{2} in expectation yields

{m}_{t,j}=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]-2\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{a}}_{j},{\bm{r}}_{j}\rangle]+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{r}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{w}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}].

Define the exact fourth-order key moments \kappa_{ji}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}^{2}{k}_{t,i}^{2}] for i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,d_{k}\}. Because \|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,i}^{2}=1 identically at every timestep t, summing \kappa_{ji} over i gives the exact marginal identity

\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}\kappa_{ji}=\mathbb{E}\!\left[{k}_{t,j}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}{k}_{t,i}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}^{2}]={c}_{j},\qquad\text{so that}\quad 0\leq\kappa_{jj}\leq{c}_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i\neq j}\kappa_{ji}={c}_{j}-\kappa_{jj}.

Conditioning on {\bm{S}}_{t-1} and applying mutual independence of ({\bm{k}}_{t},\hat{\bm{v}}_{t},{\bm{\alpha}}_{t},{\bm{b}}_{t}) together with \mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,i}{k}_{t,j}]=\delta_{ij}{c}_{j} and \mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{S}}_{t-1,i,:},{\bm{S}}_{t-1,i^{\prime},:}\rangle]=\delta_{ii^{\prime}}{m}_{t-1,i} (which eliminates all off-diagonal cross-row inner products i\neq j in \mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{a}}_{j},{\bm{r}}_{j}\rangle] and i\neq i^{\prime} in \mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{r}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]), the four terms evaluate exactly to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[{\alpha}_{t,j}^{2}]\,\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{S}}_{t-1,j,:}\|_{2}^{2}]={\lambda}_{j}{m}_{t-1,j},\qquad\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{w}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}^{2}]\,\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}]={c}_{j}{\sigma}_{v}^{2},
\displaystyle-2\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{a}}_{j},{\bm{r}}_{j}\rangle]\displaystyle=-2\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}\mathbb{E}[{\alpha}_{t,j}{\alpha}_{t,i}{b}_{t,i}{k}_{t,j}{k}_{t,i}]\,\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{S}}_{t-1,j,:},{\bm{S}}_{t-1,i,:}\rangle]=-2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}{m}_{t-1,j},
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{r}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]\displaystyle=\sum_{i,i^{\prime}=1}^{d_{k}}\mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}^{2}{k}_{t,i}{k}_{t,i^{\prime}}{b}_{t,i}{b}_{t,i^{\prime}}{\alpha}_{t,i}{\alpha}_{t,i^{\prime}}]\,\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{S}}_{t-1,i,:},{\bm{S}}_{t-1,i^{\prime},:}\rangle]=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}\mathbb{E}[{b}_{t,i}^{2}]\kappa_{ji}{\lambda}_{i}{m}_{t-1,i}.

Summing these four expressions yields the exact fourth-moment recurrence

{m}_{t,j}={\lambda}_{j}\big(1-2\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}+\mathbb{E}[{b}_{t,j}^{2}]\kappa_{jj}\big){m}_{t-1,j}+\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{E}[{b}_{t,i}^{2}]\kappa_{ji}{\lambda}_{i}{m}_{t-1,i}+{c}_{j}{\sigma}_{v}^{2}.

Applying the second-order closures \kappa_{ji}\approx{c}_{j}{c}_{i} and \mathbb{E}[{b}_{t,i}^{2}]\approx\bar{b}_{i}^{2} (while noting that on the diagonal i=j,\mathbb{E}[{b}_{t,j}^{2}]\kappa_{jj}={\mathcal{O}}({c}_{j}^{2}) is a second-order correction to 2\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}) gives ([4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E4 "Equation 4 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Dropping the cross-row coupling term reduces ([4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E4 "Equation 4 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) to the scalar recurrence {m}_{t,j}={\lambda}_{j}(1-2\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}){m}_{t-1,j}+{c}_{j}{\sigma}_{v}^{2}. Since {\lambda}_{j}\in(0,1) and \bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}\in(0,1) imply |{\lambda}_{j}(1-2\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j})|<1, this recurrence is a strict contraction and converges as t\to\infty to the unique fixed point {m}_{t,j}={m}_{t-1,j}={m}_{j}^{\ast} satisfying (1-{\lambda}_{j}+2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}){m}_{j}^{\ast}={c}_{j}{\sigma}_{v}^{2}, which gives ([5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Moreover, in ([4](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E4 "Equation 4 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), the cross-row sum \Sigma_{\mathrm{cross}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}\bar{b}_{i}^{2}{c}_{i}{\lambda}_{i}{m}_{i}^{\ast} is independent of j and enters through {c}_{j}({\sigma}_{v}^{2}+\Sigma_{\mathrm{cross}}), so retaining it at stationarity simply rescales {\sigma}_{v}^{2}\to\tilde{\sigma}_{v}^{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\sigma}_{v}^{2}/\big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}\bar{b}_{i}^{2}{c}_{i}^{2}{\lambda}_{i}\,\tau_{\mathrm{eff},i}(\bar{b}_{i})\big)>0 (where the denominator is strictly positive since \bar{b}_{i}^{2}{c}_{i}^{2}{\lambda}_{i}\,\tau_{\mathrm{eff},i}(\bar{b}_{i})<\frac{1}{2}\bar{b}_{i}{c}_{i} and \frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}\bar{b}_{i}{c}_{i}<1) while preserving the exact relative channel profile {m}_{j}^{\ast}\propto{c}_{j}\,\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}(\bar{b}_{j}) and the matched-filter direction in [Proposition 3.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"). ∎

### D.3 Lag-Dependent Matched-Filter Readout

###### Proof of [Proposition 3.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

(i) Substituting {\bm{S}}_{t,j,:}={a}_{\ell,j}{k}_{t-\ell,j}\hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell}+{\bm{\xi}}_{t,j} and {q}_{t,j}={k}_{t-\ell,j} into {\bm{o}}_{t}({\bm{\gamma}}) gives

{\bm{o}}_{t}({\bm{\gamma}})-\hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell}=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{\gamma}_{j}{k}_{t-\ell,j}^{2}{a}_{\ell,j}-1\right)\hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell}+\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{\gamma}_{j}{k}_{t-\ell,j}{\bm{\xi}}_{t,j}.

Because \hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell} is zero-mean with \mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell}\|_{2}^{2}]={\sigma}_{v}^{2}, independent of ({\bm{k}}_{t-\ell},{a}_{\ell,:}), and uncorrelated with {\bm{\xi}}_{t,:}, the cross term between the signal residual and the interference sum vanishes in expectation. Applying \mathbb{E}[{k}_{t-\ell,i}^{2}{k}_{t-\ell,j}^{2}]\approx{c}_{i}{c}_{j} and \mathbb{E}[{a}_{\ell,i}{a}_{\ell,j}]\approx\bar{a}_{\ell,i}\bar{a}_{\ell,j} to the squared signal term and \mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{\xi}}_{t,i},{\bm{\xi}}_{t,j}\rangle\mid{\bm{k}}_{t-\ell}]=\delta_{ij}{m}_{j}^{\ast} to the squared interference term yields ([6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E6 "Equation 6 ‣ Item 0(i) ‣ Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

(ii) Under the constraint \sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{\gamma}_{j}{c}_{j}\bar{a}_{\ell,j}=1, let {u}_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\gamma}_{j}\sqrt{\smash{{c}_{j}{m}_{j}^{\ast}}\vphantom{{m}}} and {v}_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sqrt{\smash{{c}_{j}/{m}_{j}^{\ast}}\vphantom{{m}}}\,\bar{a}_{\ell,j}. By Cauchy-Schwarz,

1=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{u}_{j}{v}_{j}\right)^{\!2}\leq\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{u}_{j}^{2}\right)\!\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{v}_{j}^{2}\right)=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{\gamma}_{j}^{2}{c}_{j}{m}_{j}^{\ast}\right)\!\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\frac{{c}_{j}\bar{a}_{\ell,j}^{2}}{{m}_{j}^{\ast}}\right),

with equality if and only if {u}_{j}\propto{v}_{j}, i.e., {\gamma}_{j}^{\ast}(\ell)\propto\bar{a}_{\ell,j}/{m}_{j}^{\ast}. Setting \partial{\mathcal{L}}_{\ell}({\bm{\gamma}})/\partial{\gamma}_{j}=0 in ([6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E6 "Equation 6 ‣ Item 0(i) ‣ Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) gives {\gamma}_{j}^{\ast}(\ell)=\kappa_{\ell}\,\bar{a}_{\ell,j}/{m}_{j}^{\ast} with \kappa_{\ell}={\sigma}_{v}^{2}\big(1+{\sigma}_{v}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}{c}_{i}\bar{a}_{\ell,i}^{2}/{m}_{i}^{\ast}\big)^{-1}, which establishes ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ Item 0(ii) ‣ Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). ∎

### D.4 Geometric Horizon Survival Profile and Fractional Power Law

###### Proof of [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

Under the geometric lag distribution \mathbb{P}(\ell=k)=(1-{\theta}){\theta}^{k-1} (k\in\{1,2,\dotsc\}) of mean horizon H=\mathbb{E}[\ell]=(1-{\theta})^{-1}>1, we have {\theta}=1-H^{-1}\in(0,1). Because {\lambda}_{j}\in(0,1) and {\theta}\in(0,1), the product {\theta}\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}<1, so the geometric series for g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[{\lambda}_{j}^{\ell\mkern-0.5mu\smash{\raisebox{0.55pt}{\scalebox{0.78}{$\scriptstyle/$}}}\mkern-0.5mu2}] converges absolutely:

g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-{\theta}){\theta}^{k-1}{\lambda}_{j}^{k\mkern-0.5mu\smash{\raisebox{0.55pt}{\scalebox{0.78}{$\scriptstyle/$}}}\mkern-0.5mu2}=(1-{\theta})\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\big({\theta}\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}\big)^{m}=\frac{(1-{\theta})\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}}{1-{\theta}\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}}.

Substituting 1-{\theta}=H^{-1} and {\theta}=(H-1)H^{-1} and multiplying numerator and denominator by H gives

g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})=\frac{\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}}{H-(H-1)\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}}=\frac{\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}}{1+(H-1)\big(1-\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}\big)}.

Applying Proposition [0(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.I2.i2 "Item 0(ii) ‣ Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") under the horizon-averaged constraint \sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{\gamma}_{j}{c}_{j}g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})=1 yields {\gamma}_{j}^{\mathrm{stat}}\propto g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})/{m}_{j}^{\ast}=g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})/{\rho}_{j}^{2}. In the decay-dominated regime 1-{\lambda}_{j}=\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}^{-1}\ll 1, substituting the first-order expansion 1-\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}\approx(2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j})^{-1} into g_{H}({\lambda}_{j}) and differentiating \log{\gamma}_{j}^{\mathrm{stat}}=\log g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})-\log\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}+\mathrm{const} with respect to \log{\rho}_{j} (\mathrm{d}\log\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}/\mathrm{d}\log{\rho}_{j}=2) gives

g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})\approx\frac{2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}}{2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}+H-1},\qquad-\frac{\mathrm{d}\log{\gamma}_{j}^{\mathrm{stat}}}{\mathrm{d}\log{\rho}_{j}}=2\!\left(1-\frac{\mathrm{d}\log g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})}{\mathrm{d}\log\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}}\right)\approx\frac{4\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}}{2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}+H-1},

which lies strictly inside (0,1) whenever \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}<(H-1)/2, completing the proof. ∎

To extend the log-slope derivation of [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") beyond the pure decay-dominated limit by retaining the key-erasure floor 2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}{c}>0 at fixed channel key energy {c}_{j}={c} and erase rate \bar{b}_{j}=\bar{b}, write \epsilon_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}1-{\lambda}_{j}\in(0,1) and retain the per-step target-erasure factor (1-\bar{b}{c}) in \bar{a}_{\ell,j}\approx\bar{a}_{1,j}^{\ell} with \bar{a}_{1,j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sqrt{{\lambda}_{j}}(1-\bar{b}{c}) ([Proposition 3.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). For \epsilon_{j}\ll 1 and \bar{b}{c}\ll 1, we have 1-\bar{a}_{1,j}\approx(\epsilon_{j}+2\bar{b}{c})/2\approx(2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j})^{-1} from \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}=(\epsilon_{j}+2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}{c})^{-1}\approx(\epsilon_{j}+2\bar{b}{c})^{-1} in ([5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), so the horizon-averaged signal survival \tilde{g}_{H}(\bar{a}_{1,j})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[\bar{a}_{1,j}^{\ell}]=\bar{a}_{1,j}/\big(1+(H-1)(1-\bar{a}_{1,j})\big) takes the same functional form in \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}:

\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}\approx(\epsilon_{j}+2\bar{b}{c})^{-1},\qquad\tilde{g}_{H}(\bar{a}_{1,j})\approx\frac{2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}}{2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}+H-1}.

Because {m}_{j}^{\ast}={\rho}_{j}^{2}={c}{\sigma}_{v}^{2}\,\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j} at fixed {c}, we have \mathrm{d}\log\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}/\mathrm{d}\log{\rho}_{j}=2, and differentiating \log{\gamma}_{j}^{\mathrm{stat}}=\log\tilde{g}_{H}(\bar{a}_{1,j})-\log\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}+\mathrm{const} with respect to \log{\rho}_{j} via the chain rule yields the exact same local power-law exponent for all \bar{b}{c}>0:

p(\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-\frac{\mathrm{d}\log{\gamma}_{j}^{\mathrm{stat}}}{\mathrm{d}\log{\rho}_{j}}=2\left(1-\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\tilde{g}_{H}(\bar{a}_{1,j})}{\mathrm{d}\log\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}}\right)\approx\frac{4\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}}{2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}+H-1}\in(0,2),

which lies strictly in (0,1) across all channels whose effective memory horizon satisfies \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}<(H-1)/2.

### D.5 Erase-Readout Substitution

###### Proof of [Proposition 3.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem10 "Proposition 3.10 (Erase-readout substitution). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

For each channel j, differentiating \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}(\bar{b})=(1-{\lambda}_{j}+2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}{c}_{j})^{-1} with respect to \bar{b} yields

\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}^{\prime}(\bar{b})=-\frac{2{\lambda}_{j}{c}_{j}}{(1-{\lambda}_{j}+2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}{c}_{j})^{2}}<0,\qquad\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{b})=\frac{8{\lambda}_{j}^{2}{c}_{j}^{2}}{(1-{\lambda}_{j}+2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}{c}_{j})^{3}}>0,

so \bar{\tau}(\bar{b})=S({\bm{1}})^{-2}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{c}_{j}^{2}\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}(\bar{b}) is strictly decreasing (\bar{\tau}^{\prime}(\bar{b})<0) and strictly convex (\bar{\tau}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{b})>0) on (0,2). By [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), among all {\bm{\gamma}} satisfying S({\bm{\gamma}})=S({\bm{1}}), the static matched filter {\gamma}_{j}^{\ast}\propto g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})/{m}_{j}^{\ast}(\bar{b}) strictly minimizes the numerator of {\mathcal{I}}(\bar{b},{\bm{\gamma}}) whenever {m}_{j}^{\ast}(\bar{b})/g_{H}({\lambda}_{j}) varies across j, which gives \Phi^{\ast}={\mathcal{I}}(\bar{b},{\bm{\gamma}}^{\ast})/\bar{\tau}(\bar{b})<1. At an interior minimizer \bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi), the first-order optimality condition is \Phi\,\bar{\tau}^{\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi))+B^{\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi))=0. Implicitly differentiating with respect to \Phi>0 gives

\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi)}{\mathrm{d}\Phi}=-\frac{\bar{\tau}^{\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi))}{\Phi\,\bar{\tau}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi))+B^{\prime\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi))}>0,

since \bar{\tau}^{\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast})<0,\bar{\tau}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast})>0, and B^{\prime\prime}(\bar{b}^{\ast})\geq 0. Because \bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi) is strictly increasing in \Phi and \Phi^{\ast}<1, we obtain \bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi^{\ast})<\bar{b}^{\ast}(1). Since \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}(\bar{b}) is strictly decreasing in \bar{b} for every j, the inequality \tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(\Phi^{\ast}))>\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}(\bar{b}^{\ast}(1)) follows immediately, which completes the proof. ∎

## Appendix E Synthetic Memory Experiment Details

This appendix provides the complete experimental protocols and numerical results for the two controlled memory tasks in §[3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.SS2 "3.2 Synthetic Memory Experiments ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

### E.1 Single-Head Stationary Retrieval

We simulate a single recurrent head with d_{k}=d_{v}=64 under the stationary stream conditions of §[3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.SS1 "3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"). To model the anisotropic, heavy-tailed channel key energy after the \mathrm{SiLU} nonlinearity in trained language models, we set power-law weights \tilde{c}_{j}\propto(65-j)^{-0.25} for j\in\{1,\dotsc,64\} with \sum_{j=1}^{64}\tilde{c}_{j}=1. At each timestep t, we draw {g}_{t,j}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1) i.i.d., form post-\mathrm{SiLU}-like coordinates {u}_{t,j}=\sqrt{\tilde{c}_{j}}\,(\sign({g}_{t,j})|{g}_{t,j}|^{1.6}+0.22), and normalize {\bm{k}}_{t}={\bm{u}}_{t}/\|{\bm{u}}_{t}\|_{2}\in\mathbb{S}^{63}, yielding mean key participation ratio \mathbb{E}[\eta_{\mathrm{eff}}({\bm{k}}_{t})]=\mathbb{E}[1/\|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{4}^{4}]=12.24 and channel second moments {c}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}^{2}]. Values are drawn i.i.d. as {\bm{v}}_{t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},d_{v}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{v}}) so that {\sigma}_{v}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}]=1. The decay profile {\bm{\alpha}}\in(0,1)^{1\times 64} uses retention timescales \tau_{j} log-spaced over [1.2,600] ({\alpha}_{j}=\exp(-1/\tau_{j})\in[0.435,0.9983], with {\lambda}_{j}={\alpha}_{j}^{2}), and the state evolves as {\bm{S}}_{t}=(\mathbf{I}_{64}-{\mu}{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}{\bm{k}}_{t})\Diag({\bm{\alpha}}){\bm{S}}_{t-1}+{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{t} over 3{,}000 burn-in and 30{,}000 evaluation steps across 20 random seeds.

At each evaluation step t, we sample a retrieval lag \ell_{t}\sim p(\ell), query the state with {\bm{q}}_{t}={\bm{k}}_{t-\ell_{t}} and readout gain {\bm{\gamma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times{64}} to form {\bm{o}}_{t}({\bm{\gamma}})=({\bm{\gamma}}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t-\ell_{t}}){\bm{S}}_{t}, and measure normalized retrieval MSE \mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{o}}_{t}({\bm{\gamma}})-{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}]/{\sigma}_{v}^{2}. For the geometric-horizon sweep across {\mu}\in\{0.1,0.2,\dotsc,1.9\} and H\in\{10,27,81\}, lags follow p(\ell)\propto(1-q)^{\ell-6} on \ell\in\{6,\dotsc,1{,}024\} with q=1/(H-5) (mean H); for the bimodal mixture at {\mu}={\mu}_{\mathrm{unit}}^{\ast}=0.60, we draw \ell_{t}\in\{2,80\} with equal probability. Because each analytical profile \tilde{\bm{\gamma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times{64}} specifies only the relative weighting across channels up to the global shrinkage scalar \kappa_{\ell} in ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ Item 0(ii) ‣ Proposition 3.6 (Lag-dependent matched-filter readout). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), we multiply \tilde{\bm{\gamma}} by \kappa^{\ast}=\argmin_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}[\|\kappa{\bm{o}}_{t}(\tilde{\bm{\gamma}})-{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}] fitted on the even-t evaluation steps (15{,}000 steps) and evaluated without refitting on the disjoint odd-t split (15{,}000 steps); the unconstrained 64-parameter least-squares static oracle {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{LS}} and lag-conditioned oracle {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{LS}}(\ell) are fitted and evaluated on the same disjoint split. [Table 6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A5.T6 "In E.1 Single-Head Stationary Retrieval ‣ Appendix E Synthetic Memory Experiment Details ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the complete stationary row-energy verification, geometric-horizon retrieval sweep across H\in\{10,27,81\}, and bimodal two-lag mixture (\ell_{t}\in\{2,80\}) results.

Table 6: Single-head stationary row-energy verification, geometric-horizon retrieval sweep, and bimodal two-lag readout comparison. All results are reported as \text{mean}\pm\text{SD} across 20 random seeds for a single recurrent head with d_{k}=d_{v}=64. (Top) Verification of the closed-form row-energy law at {\mu}=0.60. (Middle) Optimal erase strength {\mu}^{\ast} and normalized retrieval MSE under geometric lag distributions of mean H\in\{10,27,81\}. (Bottom) Retrieval MSE on short (\ell=2), long (\ell=80), and mixed (50/50) queries at {\mu}=0.60, along with the Spearman correlation r_{s}({\bm{\gamma}},{\bm{\alpha}}) between readout gain {\bm{\gamma}} and the channel decay factor {\bm{\alpha}}.

### E.2 Learned Multi-Query Associative Recall

We train a single-layer, two-head gated delta model (N_{h}=2,d_{k}=d_{v}=32,N_{h}d_{k}=64, residual width d=128) end-to-end on streaming multi-query associative recall [[Arora et al., 2023a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib3)] with overwriting writes. Each sequence consists of T=256 write/read events (each encoded as a single composite input vector) over a vocabulary of K=256 keys and V=64 values. For each sequence, we sample K_{\mathrm{live}}=96 active keys (1.5\times the total state rank N_{h}d_{k}=64) and assign them \zipf(1.0) occurrence probabilities. Each timestep t\in\{1,\dotsc,256\} is independently chosen as a write event with probability 0.7 (binding a sampled active key to a uniformly drawn value in \{1,\dotsc,V\} and overwriting any previous value stored for that key) or a read event with probability 0.3 (querying a previously written active key at event lag \ell_{t}=t-t_{\mathrm{last}}\in[1,256) since its most recent write).

To require subspace-selective value gating amid background residual-stream activity, we partition the V=64 values evenly across four orthogonal d_{\mathrm{sub}}-dimensional subspaces \{{\bm{Q}}_{m}\}_{m=1}^{4} (d_{\mathrm{sub}}=16,{\bm{Q}}_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{16\times 128},{\bm{Q}}_{m}{\bm{Q}}_{m^{\prime}}^{\top}=\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}\mathbf{I}_{16}, spanning 64 dimensions of \mathbb{R}^{1\times{128}}). On write steps, value v\in\{1,\dotsc,V\} activates its assigned subspace m(v)\in\{1,\dotsc,4\} with {\bm{e}}_{v}^{\mathrm{sub}}{\bm{Q}}_{m\smash{\raisebox{0.48pt}{\scalebox{0.80}{$\scriptstyle($}}}v\smash{\raisebox{0.48pt}{\scalebox{0.80}{$\scriptstyle)$}}}}+{\bm{e}}_{m\smash{\raisebox{0.48pt}{\scalebox{0.80}{$\scriptstyle($}}}v\smash{\raisebox{0.48pt}{\scalebox{0.80}{$\scriptstyle)$}}}}^{\mathrm{cat}}, while inactive subspaces m\neq m(v) carry scaled Gaussian noise {\sigma}_{\mathrm{bg}}\sqrt{d/d_{\mathrm{sub}}}\,{\bm{z}}_{t,m}{\bm{Q}}_{m} ({\bm{z}}_{t,m}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{16}),{\sigma}_{\mathrm{bg}}=0.3); on read steps, all four subspaces carry only this background noise. Adding the key and write/read role embeddings forms the pre-RMS input ({\bm{E}}_{\mathrm{key}}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times 128},{\bm{E}}_{\mathrm{role}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 128}, and {\bm{E}}_{\mathrm{cat}}\in\mathbb{R}^{4\times 128} initialized from {\mathcal{N}}(0,1), and {\bm{E}}_{\mathrm{val}}^{\mathrm{sub}}\in\mathbb{R}^{64\times 16} from {\mathcal{N}}(0,d/d_{\mathrm{sub}}) so \mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{e}}_{v}^{\mathrm{sub}}{\bm{Q}}_{m(v)}\|_{2}^{2}]=d), while queries and keys apply \mathrm{SiLU} before \ell_{2} normalization. All linear projections ({\bm{W}}_{q},{\bm{W}}_{k},{\bm{W}}_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{128\times 64},{\bm{W}}_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{R}^{64\times 64}, dense {\bm{W}}_{b},{\bm{W}}_{w}, and bottleneck factors {\bm{W}}_{m\downarrow},{\bm{W}}_{m\uparrow}) use fan-in initialization {\mathcal{N}}(0,d_{\mathrm{in}}^{-1}) with zero gate biases ({\bm{c}}_{b}={\bm{c}}_{w}={\bm{0}}), except the rank-8 decay gate ({\bm{W}}_{f\uparrow}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,0.01r_{f}^{-1}),\exp({\bm{a}}_{\log})\sim\mathrm{Unif}(1,16),\mathrm{softplus}({\bm{c}}_{f})\sim\mathrm{LogUnif}(10^{-3},10^{-1})) and readout gain (r_{q}=8,{\bm{W}}_{q\uparrow}={\bm{0}},{\bm{c}}_{q}={\bm{0}}, so {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}={\bm{1}} initially). All models are trained for 1{,}000 steps at batch size 32 using Adam (peak \text{LR}=4\times 10^{-3} with 100-step linear warmup and cosine decay to 8\times 10^{-5}, global gradient-norm clip 1.0) across 20 random seeds and evaluated on 256 held-out sequences (\approx 19{,}700 read queries per seed) partitioned into short (\ell_{t}\in[8,32)), medium (\ell_{t}\in[32,128)), and long (\ell_{t}\in[128,256)) event-lag buckets.

[Table 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A5.T7 "In E.2 Learned Multi-Query Associative Recall ‣ Appendix E Synthetic Memory Experiment Details ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports all 16 gate-rank and readout-gain configurations, alongside the token-dependent erase-logit variance fraction on write steps ([Remark 3.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Remark 3.3 (Regularization of oblique erase variance by low-rank projections). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) and mean terminal row energy.

Table 7: Learned multi-query associative recall accuracy, erase-logit token variance, and state row energy across gate-rank and readout-gain configurations. All results are evaluated on a two-head recurrent model with d_{k}=d_{v}=32 and residual width d=128 across 20 random seeds (mean \pm SD; dense gates have full head-channel rank N_{h}d_{k}=64). We report read accuracy overall and by event lag across single-gate rank sweeps and readout-gain variants with low-rank (b,w) at r_{b}=r_{w}=4 (r_{q}=8 for dynamic {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}), along with the token-dependent fraction of projected erase-logit energy on write steps \mathbb{E}[\|\tilde{\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}-\bar{\bm{z}}^{b}\|_{2}^{2}]/\mathbb{E}[\|\tilde{\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}] (\tilde{\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}={\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}-{\bm{c}}_{b}) and mean terminal row energy \bar{m}_{T}=(N_{h}d_{k})^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{S}}_{T}\|_{F}^{2}].

## Appendix F Chunkwise Kernel Analysis

Table 8: Per-token, per-head FLOP breakdown of the boundary-pivoted chunkwise kernel and naive chunkwise execution. Stage rows report leading per-token MXU and VPU operation counts (given cumulative log-decay {\bm{\Lambda}}) as a function of chunk length C, head dimensions (d_{k},d_{v}), leaf size n_{0}, and tree depth r=\log_{2}(C/n_{0}). MXU shares and totals are evaluated at C=256,d_{k}=d_{v}=128, and n_{0}=8 (r=5).

### F.1 MXU and VPU FLOP Breakdown

[Table 8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A6.T8 "In Appendix F Chunkwise Kernel Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") compares the per-token, per-head MXU and VPU operation counts of our boundary-pivoted chunkwise kernel (§[4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.SS2 "4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) against naive chunkwise execution, partitioning {\bm{T}} and {\bm{M}} into the N_{\mathrm{leaf}}=C/n_{0} diagonal blocks of size n_{0}\times n_{0} and the off-diagonal blocks across the r=\log_{2}(C/n_{0}) merge levels. Naive chunkwise execution evaluates the off-diagonal blocks via the 3D decayed-key tensor on the VPU and solves {\bm{T}}{\bm{Z}}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\bm{V}}&\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}\end{bmatrix} via sequential forward and backward substitution on the VPU. In our kernel, at merge level m\in\{0,\dotsc,r-1\} (n_{m}=n_{0}2^{m},N_{m}=C/(2n_{m}) merges per chunk), forming the pivoted cross-blocks ({\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}},{\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{cross}}) costs 2n_{m}d_{k} MXU FLOPs per token (with {\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}},{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}} evaluated on the VPU) and merging {\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{blk}}^{-1} via ([11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E11 "Equation 11 ‣ Divide-and-Conquer Block Decomposition. ‣ 4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) costs 2n_{m}^{2} MXU FLOPs per token, which sum over \sum_{m=0}^{r-1}n_{m}=C-n_{0} and \sum_{m=0}^{r-1}n_{m}^{2}=\frac{1}{3}(C^{2}-n_{0}^{2}) in the forward pass while the backward pass reuses the saved {\bm{T}}^{-1} directly.

### F.2 Chunkwise Backward Pass

Given upstream gradients \mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}\in\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{v}} for each chunk and \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{N_{c}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{v}} at the end of the sequence (where \mathrm{d}{\bm{X}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\partial{\mathcal{J}}/\partial{\bm{X}} denotes the gradient of the scalar loss {\mathcal{J}} with respect to {\bm{X}}), the backward pass proceeds in reverse order through the chunk readout and state scan, the triangular solve, and the boundary-pivoted merge tree.

##### Reverse Cross-Chunk State Scan and Readout Gradients.

In the forward pass ([10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E10 "Equation 10 ‣ 4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), each chunk advances {\bm{S}}_{0}\to({\bm{O}},{\bm{S}}_{C}) via {\bm{O}}=(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}-{\bm{M}}{\bm{Y}}){\bm{S}}_{0}+{\bm{M}}{\bm{U}} and {\bm{S}}_{C}=(\Diag({\bm{\pi}}_{C})-\tilde{\bm{K}}^{\top}{\bm{Y}}){\bm{S}}_{0}+\tilde{\bm{K}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}. Running the backward scan in reverse chunk order n=N_{c},\dotsc,1 from terminal state gradient \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C} and chunk output gradient \mathrm{d}{\bm{O}} computes

\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{0}=\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}+\Diag({\bm{\pi}}_{C})\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C}-{\bm{Y}}^{\top}({\bm{M}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}+\tilde{\bm{K}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C})

entirely in VMEM, passing \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{0} as \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C} to chunk n-1. Once {\bm{S}}_{0} and \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C} are available for all chunks, the gradients with respect to ({\bm{M}},\tilde{\bm{K}},{\bm{U}},{\bm{Y}},\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}) and {\bm{\pi}}_{C} (with \tilde{\bm{V}}={\bm{U}}-{\bm{Y}}{\bm{S}}_{0}) are computed across all chunks in parallel via packed MXU matrix multiplications:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}\displaystyle=\tril(\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}\,\tilde{\bm{V}}^{\top}),\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tilde{\bm{K}}\displaystyle=\tilde{\bm{V}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C}^{\top},\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{U}}\displaystyle={\bm{M}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}+\tilde{\bm{K}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C},
\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{Y}}\displaystyle=-\mathrm{d}{\bm{U}}\,{\bm{S}}_{0}^{\top},\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}\,{\bm{S}}_{0}^{\top},\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{C}\displaystyle={\bm{1}}_{d_{v}}\big(\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C}\odot{\bm{S}}_{0}\big)^{\top}.

Finally, differentiating the forward solve {\bm{Z}}={\bm{T}}^{-1}[\hat{\bm{V}}\;\,\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}] (where {\bm{Z}}=[{\bm{U}}\;\,{\bm{Y}}] and \mathrm{d}{\bm{Z}}=[\mathrm{d}{\bm{U}}\;\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{Y}}]) yields

\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{V}}&\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}\end{bmatrix}={\bm{T}}^{-\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{Z}},\qquad\mathrm{d}{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{strict}}=-\stril\!\Big(\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{V}}\,{\bm{U}}^{\top}+\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}{\bm{Y}}^{\top}\Big),

where {\bm{T}}^{-\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{Z}} is evaluated in a single 256\times 256 MXU matrix multiplication per chunk using the saved {\bm{T}}^{-1}.
