##### Sub-Block Merge Tree and Log-Decay Gradients.

At each merge level m\in\{0,\dotsc,r-1\} with adjacent child intervals {\mathcal{L}} and {\mathcal{R}} of length n_{m}=n_{0}2^{m} pivoted at p=\mathrm{last}({\mathcal{L}}), slicing \mathrm{d}{\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=(\mathrm{d}{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{strict}})_{{\mathcal{R}},{\mathcal{L}}} and \mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=(\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}})_{{\mathcal{R}},{\mathcal{L}}} and contracting via the stacked MXU equations in §[4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.SS2 "4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") produces \mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}}),\mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}}), and \mathrm{d}({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}}). Because {\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{R}},t}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}) for t\in{\mathcal{R}} and {\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{L}},s}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{s}) for s\in{\mathcal{L}}, the elementwise chain rule accumulates \mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathrel{+}=\mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\mathrel{+}=\mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}}, and \mathrm{d}{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\mathrel{+}=\mathrm{d}({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}})\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}} alongside the log-decay gradients \mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{R}},t}\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})_{t}\odot\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}+\mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})_{t}\odot\hat{\bm{q}}_{t},\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{L}},s}\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{d}({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}})_{s}\odot{\bm{k}}_{s},
\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{t}\displaystyle\mathrel{+}=\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{R}},t}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{R}},t}\quad(t\in{\mathcal{R}}),\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{s}\displaystyle\mathrel{-}=\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{L}},s}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{L}},s}\quad(s\in{\mathcal{L}}),

with the pivot row p=\mathrm{last}({\mathcal{L}}) receiving the net boundary flux \mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}\mathrel{+}=\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{L}}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{L}},s}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{L}},s}-\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{R}}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{R}},t}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{{\mathcal{R}},t}. Finally, adding the prefix/suffix contributions from \hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi},\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi},\tilde{\bm{K}}, and {\bm{\pi}}_{C} into (\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}) and multiplying by the transposed lower-triangular all-ones matrix on the MXU converts \mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}} into \mathrm{d}\log{\bm{\alpha}}=\tril({\bm{1}}_{C\times C})^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}=\triu({\bm{1}}_{C\times C})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}} (with rows \mathrm{d}\log{\bm{\alpha}}_{t}=\sum_{u=t}^{C}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{u}; see [Algorithms 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#alg1 "In Sub-Block Merge Tree and Log-Decay Gradients. ‣ F.2 Chunkwise Backward Pass ‣ Appendix F Chunkwise Kernel Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and[2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#alg2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ Sub-Block Merge Tree and Log-Decay Gradients. ‣ F.2 Chunkwise Backward Pass ‣ Appendix F Chunkwise Kernel Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), reference JAX implementation in [Appendix J](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A10 "Appendix J Reference Implementation ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

Algorithm 1 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Forward Pass

1:\hat{\bm{Q}},{\bm{K}},\hat{\bm{K}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{c}\times C\times d_{k}},\hat{\bm{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{c}\times C\times d_{v}},\log{\bm{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{c}\times C\times d_{k}} (C=256,n_{0}=8,r=\log_{2}(C/n_{0})=5)

2:{\bm{O}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{c}\times C\times d_{v}}

3: Compute {\bm{\Lambda}}=\tril({\bm{1}}_{C\times C})\,\log{\bm{\alpha}}, prefix decay {\bm{\pi}}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}), suffix decay \tilde{\bm{\pi}} with rows {\bm{\pi}}_{C,s}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{C}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{s})

4: On 2^{r} leaves of size n_{0}, compute {\bm{\pi}}_{t,s}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{s}) (t\geq s), ({\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{leaf}})_{t,s}=\langle\hat{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{\pi}}_{t,s}\odot{\bm{k}}_{s}\rangle (t>s), and ({\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{leaf}})_{t,s}=\langle\hat{\bm{q}}_{t},{\bm{\pi}}_{t,s}\odot{\bm{k}}_{s}\rangle (t\geq s)

5: Invert diagonal leaf blocks {\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{leaf}}^{-1}=(\mathbf{I}_{n_{0}}+{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{leaf}})^{-1}=\prod_{j=0}^{\log_{2}n_{0}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{n_{0}}+(-{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{leaf}})^{2^{j}})

6:for merge level m=0,\dotsc,r-1 (adjacent child intervals {\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{R}} of size n_{m}=n_{0}2^{m}, pivot p=\mathrm{last}({\mathcal{L}})) do

7: Compute boundary-pivot decays {\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{\mathcal{R}}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}) and {\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\mathcal{L}})

8: Compute off-diagonal blocks {\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=(\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}})^{\top} and {\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}})^{\top}

9: Merge off-diagonal inverse block ({\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{blk}}^{-1})_{{\mathcal{R}},{\mathcal{L}}}=-{\bm{T}}_{\mathcal{R}}^{-1}{\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}{\bm{T}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1} ([11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E11 "Equation 11 ‣ Divide-and-Conquer Block Decomposition. ‣ 4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"))

10:end for

11: Compute \hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}={\bm{\pi}}\odot\hat{\bm{K}},\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}={\bm{\pi}}\odot\hat{\bm{Q}}, and \tilde{\bm{K}}=\tilde{\bm{\pi}}\odot{\bm{K}} (with rows \tilde{\bm{K}}_{s,:}={\bm{\pi}}_{C,s}\odot{\bm{k}}_{s})

12: Compute {\bm{Z}}=[{\bm{U}}\;\,{\bm{Y}}]={\bm{T}}^{-1}[\hat{\bm{V}}\;\,\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}]

13: Compute {\bm{P}}_{\mathrm{read}}=[\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}-{\bm{M}}{\bm{Y}};\;\Diag({\bm{\pi}}_{C})-\tilde{\bm{K}}^{\top}{\bm{Y}}] and {\bm{P}}_{\mathrm{bias}}=[{\bm{M}}{\bm{U}};\;\tilde{\bm{K}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}] ([10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S4.E10 "Equation 10 ‣ 4.2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Kernel ‣ 4 Muir ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"))

14: Initialize recurrent state {\bm{S}}={\bm{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{v}} in VMEM

15:for n=1,\dotsc,N_{c}do

16: Save chunk-initial state {\bm{S}}_{0,[n]}={\bm{S}} and update [{\bm{O}}_{[n]};\;{\bm{S}}]\leftarrow{\bm{P}}_{\mathrm{read},[n]}{\bm{S}}+{\bm{P}}_{\mathrm{bias},[n]}

17:end for

18:return{\bm{O}}=\{{\bm{O}}_{[1]},\dotsc,{\bm{O}}_{[N_{c}]}\}

Algorithm 2 Boundary-Pivoted Chunkwise Custom Backward Pass

1: Saved {\bm{T}}^{-1},{\bm{M}},{\bm{Z}}=[{\bm{U}}\;\,{\bm{Y}}], chunk-initial states {\bm{S}}_{0}=\{{\bm{S}}_{0,[n]}\}_{n=1}^{N_{c}}, inputs (\hat{\bm{Q}},{\bm{K}},\hat{\bm{K}},\log{\bm{\alpha}}), and \mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}

2:\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{V}},\mathrm{d}\log{\bm{\alpha}}

3: Compute {\bm{\Lambda}}=\tril({\bm{1}}_{C\times C})\log{\bm{\alpha}},{\bm{\pi}}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}),\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}={\bm{\pi}}\odot\hat{\bm{Q}}, and \tilde{\bm{K}} with rows {\bm{\pi}}_{C,s}\odot{\bm{k}}_{s} (with {\bm{\pi}}_{C}={\bm{\pi}}_{C,:})

4: Initialize terminal state gradient \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}={\bm{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{v}} in VMEM

5:for n=N_{c},\dotsc,1 do

6: Save \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C,[n]}=\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}} and update \mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}\leftarrow\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi,[n]}^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}_{[n]}+\Diag({\bm{\pi}}_{C,[n]})\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}-{\bm{Y}}_{[n]}^{\top}({\bm{M}}_{[n]}^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}_{[n]}+\tilde{\bm{K}}_{[n]}\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}})

7:end for

8: Compute \tilde{\bm{V}}={\bm{U}}-{\bm{Y}}{\bm{S}}_{0},\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}=\tril(\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}\,\tilde{\bm{V}}^{\top}), and \mathrm{d}\tilde{\bm{K}}=\tilde{\bm{V}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C}^{\top}

9: Compute \mathrm{d}{\bm{U}}={\bm{M}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}+\tilde{\bm{K}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C},\mathrm{d}{\bm{Y}}=-\mathrm{d}{\bm{U}}\,{\bm{S}}_{0}^{\top},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi}=\mathrm{d}{\bm{O}}\,{\bm{S}}_{0}^{\top}, and \mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{C}={\bm{1}}_{d_{v}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{S}}_{C}\odot{\bm{S}}_{0})^{\top}

10: Compute [\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{V}}\;\,\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}]={\bm{T}}^{-\top}[\mathrm{d}{\bm{U}}\;\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{Y}}] and \mathrm{d}{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{strict}}=-\stril(\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{V}}\,{\bm{U}}^{\top}+\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}{\bm{Y}}^{\top})

11: Differentiate 2^{r} diagonal leaves \mathrm{d}{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{leaf}}=(\mathrm{d}{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{strict}})_{\mathrm{leaf}} and \mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{leaf}}=(\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}})_{\mathrm{leaf}} into (\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}})

12:for merge level m=0,\dotsc,r-1 (adjacent child intervals {\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{R}} of size n_{m}=n_{0}2^{m}, pivot p=\mathrm{last}({\mathcal{L}})) do

13: Recompute boundary-pivot decays {\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{\mathcal{R}}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}) and {\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}}=\exp({\bm{\Lambda}}_{p}-{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\mathcal{L}})

14: Slice \mathrm{d}{\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=(\mathrm{d}{\bm{L}}_{\mathrm{strict}})_{{\mathcal{R}},{\mathcal{L}}} and \mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=(\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}})_{{\mathcal{R}},{\mathcal{L}}}

15: Compute \mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})=\mathrm{d}{\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}}) and \mathrm{d}(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})=\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{cross}}({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}})

16: Compute \mathrm{d}({\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}})=\mathrm{d}{\bm{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})+\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}}\odot{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}})

17: Accumulate chain-rule gradients through ({\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}},{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}}) into (\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\mathcal{R}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{R}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{L}}) and (\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\mathcal{R}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\mathcal{L}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}_{p})

18:end for

19: Accumulate prefix/suffix decay gradients from (\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}}_{\odot\pi},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{\pi}}_{C}) into (\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}})

20: Compute \mathrm{d}\log{\bm{\alpha}}=\tril({\bm{1}}_{C\times C})^{\top}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}=\triu({\bm{1}}_{C\times C})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\Lambda}}

21:return\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{Q}},\mathrm{d}{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{K}},\mathrm{d}\hat{\bm{V}},\mathrm{d}\log{\bm{\alpha}}

### F.3 Training Throughput and Numerical Stability

[Table 10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A6.T10 "In Reverse Cross-Chunk State Scan and Readout Gradients. ‣ F.2 Chunkwise Backward Pass ‣ Appendix F Chunkwise Kernel Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the complete 1.34\text{B} training throughput (\mathrm{p50} over 100-step training intervals), speedup ratios, compiled XLA [[Sabne, 2020](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib97)] FLOPs, achieved model FLOP utilization (MFU), and HBM traffic for sequence lengths T\in\{2\text{K},4\text{K},8\text{K},16\text{K},32\text{K}\} on 8 TPU7x chips at a fixed global batch size B\times T=524{,}288\text{ tokens/step}. Across all Transformer runs, TPU Pallas Splash Attention uses 512-token bfloat16 query and key-value VMEM tiles, with 128-token key-value compute sub-blocks in the forward and key-value backward kernels. [Table 10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A6.T10 "In Reverse Cross-Chunk State Scan and Readout Gradients. ‣ F.2 Chunkwise Backward Pass ‣ Appendix F Chunkwise Kernel Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the forward and backward relative errors against an exact float64 sequential reference and the empirical condition number \kappa_{2}({\bm{T}}) across synthetic stress regimes and 100\text{B}-token trained checkpoints.

## Appendix G Training Setup and Model Configurations

### G.1 Pretraining Setup

Across all four model scales ([Table 11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A7.T11 "In G.1 Pretraining Setup ‣ Appendix G Training Setup and Model Configurations ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), every layer allocates 4d^{2} parameters to the core query, key, value, and output projections ({\bm{W}}_{q},{\bm{W}}_{k},{\bm{W}}_{v},{\bm{W}}_{o}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}) and 8d^{2} parameters between the gate projections and the two-matrix \mathrm{GELU} FFN (2d\,d_{\mathrm{ffn}} parameters), yielding an exact per-layer non-embedding budget of 12d^{2}:

\text{Transformer:}\;\;d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=4d;\qquad\text{GDN-2:}\;\;d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=3d-256;\qquad\text{Muir:}\;\;d_{\mathrm{ffn}}=4d-R_{\mathrm{tot}}=4d-512.

All recurrent models include a four-token depthwise causal convolution over the concatenated (q,k,v) channels (4\times 3d parameters per layer) and per-head \mathrm{RMSNorm} normalization prior to the output gate \mathrm{SiLU}({\bm{g}}_{t}).

Adapting the decay parameterization of [Yang et al. [2025b]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126) and [Hatamizadeh et al. [2026]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42), we stratify the N_{h} heads across four quartiles k\in\{1,2,3,4\} to reduce initialization variance at fixed seed:9 9 9 The (64,64,128,256,0) no-{\bm{\gamma}} runs used unstratified [1,16] and [0.001,0.1] initialization; since a_{\log} and {\bm{c}}_{f} are freely learned over 100\text{B} tokens, they match the stratified (128,128,128,128,0) no-{\bm{\gamma}} runs in [Table 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.T2 "In 5.3 Ablations ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") within 0.5\text{ mnats} at 834\text{M} and 1.34\text{B}.

\exp(a_{\log})\sim\mathrm{Unif}\big(2^{k-1},\,2^{k}\big);\qquad\mathrm{softplus}({c}_{f,j})\sim\mathrm{LogUnif}(\tau_{k-1},\,\tau_{k}),\;\;\tau_{0:4}=(0.001,0.004,0.015,0.05,0.15).

The remaining up-projection biases ({\bm{c}}_{g} inherited from GDN-2, and {\bm{c}}_{b},{\bm{c}}_{w},{\bm{c}}_{q} newly added in Muir) are initialized to {\bm{0}}. In Muir, the dynamic readout up-projection is initialized to {\bm{W}}_{q\uparrow}={\bm{0}} and the static readout gain to {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}}={\bm{1}} so that 2\sigma({\bm{z}}^{q}_{t})={\bm{1}} and {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}={\bm{1}} at initialization (with zero weight decay on a_{\log}, additive biases {\bm{c}}_{m}, and {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}}). All pretraining runs across all scales use a fixed random seed of 0 for both model initialization and deterministic data-shard ordering, and all models and the boundary-pivoted chunkwise kernel are implemented in JAX [[Bradbury et al., 2018](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib20)] and compiled with XLA [[Sabne, 2020](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib97)].

Table 11: Architectural specifications and parameter accounting across all four iso-parameter scales (\bm{166\text{M}} to \bm{1.34\text{B}}). All models at a given scale share depth L, residual width d, head dimensions d_{k}=d_{v}=128, head count N_{h}=d/128 (so all recurrent models are strictly iso-state with N_{h}d_{k}d_{v}=128d state elements per layer), vocabulary size V=32{,}000 (untied input/output embeddings), per-layer non-embedding budget 12d^{2}, global batch size 524{,}288\text{ tokens/step} (B=128,T=4{,}096), and 100\text{B} training tokens.

### G.2 Long-Context Cooldown and Position Extrapolation

For the 64\text{K} continual cooldown at 1.34\text{B} parameters, we initialize model weights from the 100\text{B}-token checkpoints with fresh optimizer state and train for 300 steps at sequence length T=65{,}536 and global batch size 1{,}048{,}576\text{ tokens/step} (16 sequences sharded across 16 TPU7x TensorCores via fully sharded data parallelism, FSDP), totaling 314{,}572{,}800 tokens (0.315\text{B}, or 0.3\% of pretraining). Following [Fu et al. [2024]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib34), the cooldown stream is drawn from FineWeb-Edu shards disjoint from both pretraining and evaluation data, using a 70/30 token mixture of intact long documents (\geq\!16{,}384 tokens) and standard documents (<\!16{,}384 tokens) without synthetic retrieval examples. Optimization uses AdamW (\beta_{1}=0.9,\beta_{2}=0.999, weight decay 0.1, gradient norm clip 1.0) with a 30-step (10\%) linear warmup to 10^{-4} and cosine decay to 10^{-5}.

Recurrent models keep identical parameterization and hyperparameters across context lengths, whereas Transformer raises its RoPE base from \theta=10^{4} to 5\times 10^{5} during cooldown [[Xiong et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib121)]. At evaluation lengths T>T_{0} beyond the active training window (T_{0}=4{,}096 zero-shot and T_{0}=65{,}536 post-cooldown), our Transformer baseline applies training-free Dynamic YaRN [[Peng et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib85)] with scale factor s(T)=T/T_{0}, NTK-by-parts rotation thresholds (\beta_{\mathrm{slow}},\beta_{\mathrm{fast}})=(1,32) across rotary dimension pairs, and query-key rotary amplitude scale 0.1\ln s(T)+1.

## Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results

### H.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning

All perplexity and zero-shot commonsense evaluations in [Tables 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.T1 "In Pretraining and Scaling Dynamics. ‣ 5.2.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and[2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.T2 "Table 2 ‣ 5.3 Ablations ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") are executed via the lm-evaluation-harness (v0.4.11; [Gao et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib35)) with float32 output logits, rolling log-likelihood context window 2{,}048 (and 4{,}096 in [Table 12](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T12 "In H.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) for WikiText, and length-normalized accuracy (acc n) for HellaSwag and ARC-Challenge [[Yang et al., 2025b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126), [Lahoti et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib63)]. As noted in §[5.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.SS1 "5.1 Experimental Setup ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), the BoolQ validation split (3{,}270 binary yes/no passages) has a 62.2\% majority-class (“yes”) baseline [[Clark et al., 2019](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib24)]. For language models below 1.5\text{B} parameters trained on 100\text{B} tokens, log-likelihood margins between “yes” and “no” cluster near zero, so small shifts in decision threshold flip hundreds of predictions and induce 5\text{--}8 percentage points of single-checkpoint variance that is uncorrelated with held-out validation NLL [[Madaan et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib71), [Miller, 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib75), [Heineman et al., 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib43)]. Complementing the FineWeb-Edu validation NLL, 2\text{K} WikiText and LAMBADA perplexities, and 8-task zero-shot commonsense average in [Table 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.T1 "In Pretraining and Scaling Dynamics. ‣ 5.2.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), [Table 12](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T12 "In H.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports FineWeb-Edu validation token perplexity, full-context (T=4{,}096) WikiText word perplexity, zero-shot BoolQ accuracy, and the 9-task zero-shot average including BoolQ (“Avg. (9)”) across all four scales.

Table 12: Supplementary language modeling perplexities, zero-shot BoolQ accuracy, and 9-task commonsense average across all four scales. Complementing [Table 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.T1 "In Pretraining and Scaling Dynamics. ‣ 5.2.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), we report FineWeb-Edu validation token perplexity (PPL), full-window (T=4{,}096) WikiText word-level perplexity, zero-shot BoolQ accuracy, and the 9-task zero-shot commonsense average including BoolQ (“Avg. (9)”).

### H.2 Associative Recall

##### RULER Benchmark Protocol.

For synthetic associative retrieval on RULER [[Hsieh et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib49)], we evaluate six tasks across context lengths T\in\{1\text{K},2\text{K},4\text{K},8\text{K}\} (N=500 examples per task and context length, generated with fixed random seed 42 and tokenized with the Llama-2 vocabulary, reserving 64 tokens for generation so each input prompt contains at most T-64 tokens). For each task and context length, target needles are inserted across 40 uniformly spaced relative depth positions d\in[0,1] from the start (0\%) to the end (100\%) of the haystack.

Table 13: Complete synthetic associative recall accuracy on the six RULER benchmarks at \bm{1.34\text{B}} parameters. (Top) Single-needle retrieval on S-NIAH-1/2/3, and their 3-task mean. (Bottom) Multi-hop VT, multi-value (MV-NIAH) and multi-query (MQ-NIAH) retrieval, and suite-wide averages across multi-needle retrieval (MV+MQ), all six tasks at 1\text{K}\text{--}4\text{K}, and all evaluated lengths (1\text{K}\text{--}8\text{K}).

On single-needle retrieval (S-NIAH-1/2/3), each example embeds a single needle of the form "One of the special magic {type} for {key} is: {value}." inside a background haystack of repetitive noise sentences (on S-NIAH-1) or concatenated Paul Graham essays (on S-NIAH-2 and S-NIAH-3). Here, {key} is a random two-word hyphenated string (e.g., "wandering-age") and ({type}, {value}) is ("number", 7-digit integer) on S-NIAH-1/2 and ("uuid", 36-char UUID) on S-NIAH-3, formatted with the prompt template:

> A special magic {type} is hidden within the following text. Make sure to memorize it. I will quiz you about the {type} afterwards.   
> {haystack_with_needle}   
> What is the special magic {type} for {key} mentioned in the provided text? The special magic {type} for {key} mentioned in the provided text is:

On multi-value retrieval (MV-NIAH), four distinct 7-digit integer values (V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},V_{4}) bound to a single shared key are inserted in chronological write order inside a Paul Graham essay haystack ("One of the special magic numbers for {key} is: {V_{m}}.") and queried together:

> Some special magic numbers are hidden within the following text. Make sure to memorize it. I will quiz you about the numbers afterwards.   
> {haystack_with_needles}   
> What are all the special magic numbers for {key} mentioned in the provided text? The special magic numbers for {key} mentioned in the provided text are:

On multi-query retrieval (MQ-NIAH), four distinct keys K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},K_{4} paired with 7-digit integer values V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},V_{4} are embedded at four positions inside a Paul Graham essay haystack and queried jointly:

> Some special magic numbers are hidden within the following text. Make sure to memorize it. I will quiz you about the numbers afterwards.   
> {haystack_with_needles}   
> What are all the special magic numbers for {K_{1}}, {K_{2}}, {K_{3}}, and {K_{4}} mentioned in the provided text? The special magic numbers for {K_{1}}, {K_{2}}, {K_{3}}, and {K_{4}} mentioned in the provided text are:

On variable tracking (VT), following [Hsieh et al. [2024]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib49), each prompt prepends a fixed one-shot demonstration of a 5-hop variable-binding chain in noise text, followed by a test passage of repetitive noise sentences containing a target chain of 5 random 5-letter uppercase variable names (\texttt{VAR\_1},\dotsc,\texttt{VAR\_5}) bound sequentially to a 5-digit integer V across five hop statements H_{1}\text{--}H_{5} at token positions s_{1}<\dotsb<s_{5} ("VAR_1 = V", "VAR_2 = VAR_1",\dotsc,\,"VAR_5 = VAR_4") before the query token t_{\mathrm{q}}:

> Memorize and track the chain(s) of variable assignment hidden in the following text.   
> {one_shot_demonstration}   
> {haystack_with_variable_assignments}   
> Question: Find all variables that are assigned the value {V} in the text above. Answer: According to the chain(s) of variable assignment in the text above, 5 variables are assigned the value {V}, they are:

Table 14: Complete real-world extractive recall accuracy on the six Based/JRT benchmarks across all four model scales. Per-task accuracies are reported under the official 2\text{K} context-cut JRT protocol (12{,}077 validation examples across structured attribute extraction and passage question answering; the 1.34\text{B}2\text{K} context-cut average is reported in [Table 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.T2 "In 5.3 Ablations ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), alongside the 6-task average under 1\text{K} context-cut truncation and un-cut 2\text{K} left-truncation.

Decoding, Scoring, and Depth-Resolved Analysis. Under greedy decoding, following the official RULER string_match_all metric [[Hsieh et al., 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib49)], an example with reference target list {\mathcal{Y}}=(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{M}) (M=1 on S-NIAH-1/2/3, M=4 on MV-NIAH and MQ-NIAH, and M=5 on VT) and generated continuation \hat{s} receives fractional recall score

\mathrm{Score}(\hat{s},{\mathcal{Y}})=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{I}\!\left[y_{m}\in\hat{s}\right]\times 100\%.

For the depth-by-length heatmap in Figure [6 e](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.F6 "Figure 6 ‣ Short-Context Parametric Knowledge and Context-Dependent Readout. ‣ 5.2.1 Language Modeling and Commonsense Reasoning ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), each target needle y_{m} is assigned to one of five 20\% relative depth quintiles (\{0\text{--}20\%,20\text{--}40\%,40\text{--}60\%,60\text{--}80\%,80\text{--}100\%\}) according to the character offset of its insertion statement within the haystack passage (excluding the one-shot header on VT and the trailing question suffix), and each cell reports the unweighted mean across the plotted tasks at that context length (6 tasks at 1\text{K}\text{--}4\text{K} and the 4 single-needle and variable-tracking tasks at 8\text{K}). In Figure [13 a,b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), we average \mathbb{I}[y_{m}\in\hat{s}] for each target index m\in\{1,\dotsc,M\} separately across the N=500 examples to compute per-write (V_{1}\text{--}V_{4}) and per-hop (H_{1}\text{--}H_{5}) recall accuracies.

[Table 13](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T13 "In RULER Benchmark Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the complete per-task and per-length results across all six RULER tasks at 1.34\text{B} parameters, with all models (including our GDN-2 baseline) pretrained from scratch and evaluated under the same pipeline (§[5.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.SS1 "5.1 Experimental Setup ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). While our re-trained 1.34\text{B} GDN-2 baseline closely matches the language modeling perplexities and zero-shot accuracies in [Hatamizadeh et al. [2026]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42), its RULER scores differ in several cells from Table 3 of [Hatamizadeh et al. [2026]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42) for three reasons. First, we evaluate S-NIAH-1/2/3 with lm-evaluation-harness (v0.4.11), which constructs different essay haystacks and prompt lengths than the standalone NVIDIA/RULER scripts (for example, on S-NIAH-3 at 1\text{K}, our same 1.34\text{B} GDN-2 checkpoint scores 77.4\% under lm-evaluation-harness vs. 84.6\% under NVIDIA/RULER). Second, we include a trailing colon in the answer prefix ("is:"/"are:") across all tasks to match the needle format inside the haystack. Third, whereas in-distribution recall at 1\text{K} and 2\text{K} agrees within 0.8\% across both studies (99.2\% to 100.0\% on S-NIAH-1/2), zero-shot length extrapolation fluctuates substantially across runs and scales: on S-NIAH-1 at 8\text{K}, our 406\text{M} and 834\text{M} GDN-2 checkpoints reach 99.0\% and 88.8\% while our 1.34\text{B} checkpoint drops to 40.8\%, whereas on S-NIAH-2 at 8\text{K} our 1.34\text{B} checkpoint scores higher than [Hatamizadeh et al. [2026]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42) (51.6\% vs. 39.2\%).

Table 15: Zero-shot length extrapolation from \bm{4\text{K}} to \bm{128\text{K}} on contiguous FineWeb-Edu long documents and packed control streams. For each evaluation window T\in\{4\text{K},8\text{K},16\text{K},32\text{K},64\text{K},128\text{K}\}, we report aggregate token perplexity alongside the paired tail NLL differential \Delta_{\mathrm{tail}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{NLL}_{[127\mathrm{K},128\mathrm{K})}(T=128\text{K})-\mathrm{NLL}_{[127\mathrm{K},128\mathrm{K})}(T=4\text{K}) (mean \pm paired SE in nats across N_{\mathrm{docs}} sequences).

##### Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol.

For real-world extractive recall on Based/JRT [[Arora et al., 2024a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib5), [Arora et al., 2024b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib6)], [Table 14](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T14 "In RULER Benchmark Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports per-task accuracy across all four scales (12{,}077 total validation examples across SWDE, FDA, SQuAD, TriviaQA, Natural Questions, and DROP) under the official 2\text{K} context-cut protocol, which truncates passages to a 2{,}048-token window centered around the target answer span following [Yang et al. [2025b]](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib126), alongside the 6-task average under two additional context-truncation protocols: the 1\text{K} context-cut protocol (1{,}024-token window) and uncut 2\text{K} left-truncation (each raw document is left-truncated to fit the 2{,}048-token window, reserving 48 tokens for generation).

Table 16: Zero-shot and post-cooldown language-modeling extrapolation up to \bm{10\text{M}}-token streams at \bm{1.34\text{B}} parameters. Complementing [Figures 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.F7 "In Depth Horizon and Real-World Extractive Recall. ‣ 5.2.2 Associative Recall ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and[15](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T15 "Table 15 ‣ RULER Benchmark Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), we evaluate 1.34\text{B} checkpoints both zero-shot (T_{0}=4\text{K}) and after the 300-step 64\text{K} continual cooldown (T_{0}=64\text{K},0.315\text{B} tokens) on (top) 64 contiguous 128\text{K}-token FineWeb-Edu documents and (bottom) three continuous 10\text{M}-token streams _without_ EOS resets; Transformer (YaRN) applies Dynamic YaRN for T>T_{0} (\theta=5\times 10^{5} during cooldown), and unchunked evaluation exceeds HBM (OOM) at T\geq 128\text{K}.

### H.3 Length Extrapolation

##### Language-Modeling Extrapolation Protocol.

We construct three long-context evaluation corpora from held-out FineWeb-Edu validation shards disjoint from both pretraining and cooldown data: (i) N_{\mathrm{docs}}=64 single contiguous documents truncated to S=131{,}072 target tokens each (8.39\text{M} target tokens, with a single leading BOS token and no internal BOS or EOS resets); (ii) N_{\mathrm{docs}}=64 packed validation control slices of S=131{,}072 target tokens each, formed by concatenating standard validation documents separated by EOS tokens; and (iii) N_{\mathrm{streams}}=3 continuous long-document streams of S=10{,}485{,}760 (10\times 2^{20}) target tokens each (31.46\text{M} target tokens, formed from concatenated \geq\!16\text{K} documents with a single leading BOS token and internal EOS tokens stripped so recurrent state accumulation proceeds uninterrupted across 10\text{M} tokens). For each evaluation window T dividing S, each sequence is partitioned into S/T non-overlapping windows with recurrent state or KV cache reset at window boundaries so every T scores the identical target tokens; on the 128\text{K} corpora, \Delta_{\mathrm{tail}} pairs per-sequence mean NLL over the final 1{,}024 target positions [127\text{K},128\text{K}) between T=128\text{K} and T=4\text{K}.

All 1.34\text{B} evaluations run on 8 TPU7x chips (16 TensorCores) without rematerialization or chunked KV prefill: data-parallel RoPE Transformer ([Table 15](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T15 "In RULER Benchmark Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) fits through T=128\text{K}, whereas unchunked evaluation and prefill in our 16-way model-sharded harnesses ([Tables 16](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T16 "In Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and[17](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T17 "Table 17 ‣ Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) exceed HBM at T\geq 128\text{K} (OOM), while recurrent models carry a fixed 25\text{ MB}float32 state across fixed-length segments through T=10\text{M}.[Table 15](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T15 "In RULER Benchmark Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports cross-scale (166\text{M}\text{--}1.34\text{B}) zero-shot extrapolation from 4\text{K} to 128\text{K} on contiguous documents and packed control streams, and [Table 16](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T16 "In Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports 1.34\text{B} zero-shot and 64\text{K}-cooldown extrapolation across the 128\text{K} documents and 10\text{M}-token streams.

##### Long-Context Passkey Retrieval Protocol.

To evaluate passkey retrieval up to 1\text{M} tokens ([Table 17](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T17 "In Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), we construct RULER S-NIAH-1 prompts across 11 geometric context lengths T=2^{k}\times 1{,}024 for k\in\{0,\dotsc,10\} using the S-NIAH-1 template in §[H.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.SS2 "H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), reserving 128 tokens for the query suffix and 32-token greedy continuation. At each context length, N=160 examples are stratified evenly across 10 relative needle-depth bins (16 examples per 10\% depth bin from 0\% to 100\%), which directly populate the depth-by-length heatmaps in [Figure 8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S5.F8 "In 5.2.3 Length Extrapolation ‣ 5.2 Results ‣ 5 Experiments ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and the per-length accuracies in [Table 17](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T17 "In Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout").

[Table 17](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T17 "In Based/JRT Extractive Recall Protocol. ‣ H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") also evaluates zero-shot Muir with dynamic query modulation frozen at inference time: setting {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\leftarrow{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} (using {\bm{z}}^{q}_{t}\leftarrow{\bm{0}}) breaks co-adaptation with {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} by 8\text{K}\text{--}16\text{K}, while setting {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\leftarrow\bar{\bm{\gamma}} (2\sigma({\bm{z}}^{q}_{t}) fixed to its held-out text mean) preserves recall through 16\text{K} yet remains below active Muir at \geq\!32\text{K}, confirming that the \geq\!64\text{K} passkey gap reflects halved write rank (r_{w}=128 vs. 256) rather than query-gate drift.

### H.4 Architecture and Gate-Allocation Controls

[Table 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.T18 "In Language-Modeling Extrapolation Protocol. ‣ H.3 Length Extrapolation ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the parameter counts, gate-rank allocations, FineWeb-Edu validation NLL and token perplexity, and full-window (T=4{,}096) WikiText word-level perplexity for the dense dynamic-readout, fixed-FFN gate-compression, and unconstrained wide-FFN controls at the 406\text{M} scale.

## Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics

All mechanistic probes in §[6](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6 "6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and this appendix are evaluated without retraining, on the 100\text{B}-token checkpoints, over held-out FineWeb-Edu validation sequences of length T=4{,}096 (16 sequences, 65{,}536 tokens per evaluation pass). To eliminate floating-point accumulation drift when comparing closed-form stationary moments against empirical state trajectories, we execute the token-level recurrence in float64 when recording channel-wise state norms and logit sensitivities, and discard the initial burn-in window t<1{,}024 (t\in[1{,}024,4{,}096)). All counterfactual validation loss changes \Delta\text{NLL}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{NLL}_{\mathrm{intervene}}-\mathrm{NLL}_{\mathrm{base}} are paired per sequence across the held-out evaluation batch, and we report the mean difference alongside its paired standard error (SE).

### I.1 Gate-Sensitivity Diagnostics

Table 19: Post-hoc SVD gate-rank truncation sensitivity on the trained \bm{406\text{M}} uniform-rank checkpoint. For each gate m\in\{f,b,g,w,q\}, we replace its rank-128 factor {\bm{W}}_{m\downarrow}{\bm{W}}_{m\uparrow} across all 11 layers with its best rank-r SVD approximation (retaining the additive bias {\bm{c}}_{m}) and measure \Delta\text{NLL}(r)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{NLL}(r)-\mathrm{NLL}(128) (in nats \pm paired SE) on held-out FineWeb-Edu sequences.

##### Post-Hoc Gate-Rank Truncation.

To evaluate the marginal value of bottleneck rank across all five gates (f,b,g,w,q) (Figure [10 a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F10 "Figure 10 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and [Table 19](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T19 "In I.1 Gate-Sensitivity Diagnostics ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), we inspect the trained 406\text{M} uniform-rank Muir checkpoint (L=11,d=1{,}536,N_{h}=12,d_{k}=d_{v}=128), in which every gate is parameterized with an identical bottleneck rank of 128. For a single gate m\in\{f,b,g,w,q\} at a time, we compute the singular value decomposition (SVD) of its rank-128 factor {\bm{W}}_{m\downarrow}{\bm{W}}_{m\uparrow}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d} in every layer, zero out the trailing 128-r singular values for r\in\{0,16,32,48,64,96\} while keeping the learned bias {\bm{c}}_{m} intact (so r=0 reduces the gate to its static channel bias), and evaluate validation NLL with the remaining four gates fixed at rank 128. As an architecture-independent cross-check on dense gates, applying the same SVD rank truncation to the dense d\times d erase and write projections ({\bm{W}}_{b},{\bm{W}}_{w}) of the 166\text{M} GDN-2 checkpoint yields \Delta\text{NLL}_{w}/\Delta\text{NLL}_{b}=8.3\text{--}11.3\times across r\in\{32,64,128,256\} (e.g., +145.9 vs. +13.2\text{ mnats} at r=64), which confirms that the write-erase sensitivity asymmetry is intrinsic to the channel-wise delta recurrence and not an artifact of the low-rank parameterization.

##### Write/Erase Perturbations.

To verify [Proposition 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") layer by layer on the 166\text{M} checkpoints, we evaluate the expected squared Frobenius logit sensitivities \mathbb{E}[\|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{z}}^{w}}{\bm{S}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{z}}^{w}]\|_{F}^{2}] and \mathbb{E}[\|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{z}}^{b}}{\bm{S}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{z}}^{b}]\|_{F}^{2}] induced by ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ Item 0(ii) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) under isotropic pre-activation perturbations \delta{\bm{z}}^{b}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d_{k}}) and \delta{\bm{z}}^{w}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d_{v}}). Across individual layers, the write-to-erase sensitivity ratio \mathbb{E}[\|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{z}}^{w}}{\bm{S}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{z}}^{w}]\|_{F}^{2}]/\mathbb{E}[\|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{z}}^{b}}{\bm{S}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{z}}^{b}]\|_{F}^{2}] spans 2.5\times to 29.7\times, with a layer-wise geometric mean of 11.9\times in GDN-2 and 11.0\times in Muir (arithmetic mean of 13.2\times and 13.3\times). Furthermore, under \delta{\bm{b}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d_{k}}), evaluating the ratio of parallel-to-total key-erase sensitivity \mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{b}}}{\mu}_{t}[\delta{\bm{b}}])^{2}]/\mathbb{E}[\|\mathrm{D}_{{\bm{b}}}\hat{\bm{k}}_{t}[\delta{\bm{b}}]\|_{2}^{2}] reproduces the empirical fourth-moment key concentration \mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{k}}_{t}\|_{4}^{4}]=\mathbb{E}[\eta_{\mathrm{eff}}({\bm{k}}_{t})^{-1}] to within 0.3\% across all layers, which verifies Proposition [0(iii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.I1.i3 "Item 0(iii) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") on real token activations.

##### Empirical Channel-Moment Estimators and Row-Law Ablation.

To evaluate the closed-form stationary row-energy law {m}_{j}^{\ast}={c}_{j}{\sigma}_{v}^{2}/(1-{\lambda}_{j}+2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}) in ([5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) on the 166\text{M} GDN-2 and Muir (static {\bm{\gamma}}) checkpoints, we estimate for each head and channel j\in\{1,\dotsc,d_{k}\} over the post-burn-in trajectory t\in[1{,}024,4{,}096) the empirical moments {c}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[{k}_{t,j}^{2}],{\lambda}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[{\alpha}_{t,j}^{2}],\bar{b}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[{b}_{t,j}], and head-level written value energy {\sigma}_{v}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\bm{v}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}], excluding dead channels with {\rho}_{j}=\sqrt{\smash{{m}_{j}}\vphantom{{m}}}<10^{-10} (<\!1.5\% of channels). [Table 20](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T20 "In I.2 Stationary Row-Energy Law Ablation and Readout Scaling ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") compares the full key-and-erasure-corrected fixed point {m}_{j}^{\ast} ([5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proposition 3.4 (Stationary row-energy law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) against the naive decay-only approximation {m}_{j,\mathrm{naive}}^{\ast}\propto(1-{\lambda}_{j})^{-1} that omits heterogeneous key write energy {c}_{j} and diagonal erasure 2{\lambda}_{j}\bar{b}_{j}{c}_{j}. Across live channels, {m}_{j}^{\ast} achieves a median within-head log-Pearson correlation r(\log_{10}{m}_{j},\log_{10}{m}_{j}^{\ast}) of 0.951 in both GDN-2 and Muir (0.932 and 0.916 pooled across all heads), whereas dropping {c}_{j} in (1-{\lambda}_{j})^{-1} lowers median within-head log-Pearson correlation to 0.840 and 0.803 (0.869 and 0.827 pooled). This confirms that both heterogeneous decay {\lambda}_{j} and non-uniform key channel variance {c}_{j} are required to predict stationary state energy ([Remark 3.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem5 "Remark 3.5 (Two-regime scaling of stationary row energy). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

##### Cross-Scale Readout Scaling and Within-Head Alignment.

Across all heads of the 166\text{M} Muir (static {\bm{\gamma}}) checkpoint, the learned readout gain magnitude |{\gamma}_{j}| has a median within-head Spearman rank correlation with inverse stationary row RMS {\rho}_{j}^{-1} of r_{s}(|{\gamma}_{j}|,{\rho}_{j}^{-1})=+0.71, with positive alignment (r_{s}>0) in 100\% of heads (+0.706 and 98\% across live channels in uniform Muir static {\bm{\gamma}}; [Table 20](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T20 "In I.2 Stationary Row-Energy Law Ablation and Readout Scaling ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). In the 1.34\text{B} Muir model with token-dependent {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}, regressing the log time-averaged readout gain \log_{10}\bar{\gamma}_{j}=\log_{10}\mathbb{E}[{\gamma}_{t,j}] against \log_{10}{\rho}_{j} within each of the 384 heads yields a median power-law slope -\mathrm{d}\log\bar{\gamma}_{j}/\mathrm{d}\log{\rho}_{j}=0.44 (\bar{\gamma}_{j}\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-0.44}; [Table 20](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T20 "In I.2 Stationary Row-Energy Law Ablation and Readout Scaling ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), which matches the 166\text{M} static exponent p=0.35 and lies inside the fractional interval p\in(0,1) of [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"). On the amplitude (RMS) scale at 1.34\text{B}, weighting state rows by \bar{\gamma}_{j} reduces the median within-head \mathrm{p90}/\mathrm{p10} readout row-RMS ratio from 3.83\times in GDN-2 (unweighted {\rho}_{j}=\sqrt{\smash{{m}_{j}}\vphantom{{m}}}, i.e., \bar{\gamma}_{j}=1) to 3.10\times for \bar{\gamma}_{j}{\rho}_{j} in Muir (-19.1\%).

### I.3 Counterfactual Interventions and Memory-Horizon Diagnostics

Table 21: Causal gate-freezing and erase-logit shift interventions on trained \bm{834\text{M}} checkpoints. (Top) Validation \Delta\text{NLL} and mean erase rate \bar{\mu} when replacing the dynamic write gate {\bm{w}}_{t} or erase gate {\bm{b}}_{t} with its stream mean (\bar{\bm{w}} or \bar{\bm{b}}) or isotropic key-space projection ({\mu}_{t}{\bm{1}}) across all layers. (Bottom) Additive erase-gate logit shifts {\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}\leftarrow{\bm{z}}^{b}_{t}+\delta_{b} for \delta_{b}\in\{-1.0,-0.5,+0.5\}, reported overall ([0,4{,}096)) and stratified by token position bucket ([0,16),[16,256), and [256,4{,}096)).

Table 22: Counterfactual readout gain replacement on trained \bm{166\text{M}} static-\bm{{\bm{\gamma}}} checkpoints. In every head and layer, we replace the learned static readout gain {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} (which has empirical log-log slope -\mathrm{d}\log|{\gamma}_{j}|/\mathrm{d}\log{\rho}_{j}=0.354 in Muir static {\bm{\gamma}} and 0.353 in uniform Muir static {\bm{\gamma}}) with a norm-matched power-law profile {\gamma}_{j}\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-p} for p\in\{0.00,0.25,0.50,0.75,1.00\} (left) or a single-horizon [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") profile g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})/{m}_{j}^{\ast} for H\in\{16,64,256,1{,}024,4{,}096\} (right).

##### Position-Stratified Gate Freezing, Isotropic Erase, and Erase-Logit Shifts.

[Table 22](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T22 "In I.3 Counterfactual Interventions and Memory-Horizon Diagnostics ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") provides the complete position-stratified breakdown ([0,16),[16,256), and [256,4{,}096)) and induced parallel erase rates \bar{\mu} for the causal gate interventions on the 834\text{M} checkpoints (Figure [10 b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F10 "Figure 10 ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and Figure [12 b](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F12 "Figure 12 ‣ Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). In addition to temporal gate freezing ({\bm{w}}_{t}\leftarrow\bar{\bm{w}} and {\bm{b}}_{t}\leftarrow\bar{\bm{b}}), we evaluate an isotropic channel-erase intervention {\bm{b}}_{t}\leftarrow{\mu}_{t}{\bm{1}} that preserves the exact token-by-token parallel erase scalar {\mu}_{t}=\langle{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{b}}_{t}\odot{\bm{k}}_{t}\rangle while zeroing out the oblique erase residual {\bm{s}}_{t}=({\bm{b}}_{t}-{\mu}_{t}{\bm{1}})\odot{\bm{k}}_{t} (Proposition [0(iii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.I1.i3 "Item 0(iii) ‣ Proposition 3.1 (State sensitivity to erase and write gates). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and [Lemma D.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A4.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma D.1 (Spectral norm of oblique rank-one erase). ‣ D.1 First-Order Gate Sensitivity and Spectral Norm of Oblique Erase Transitions ‣ Appendix D Mathematical Proofs ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")). Zeroing out the oblique residual {\bm{s}}_{t} degrades validation NLL by only +0.084\text{ nats} in GDN-2 and +0.049\text{ nats} in Muir (8\text{--}10\times smaller than freezing {\bm{w}}_{t}; slightly above {\bm{b}}_{t}\leftarrow\bar{\bm{b}} because \bar{\bm{b}} retains static per-channel baseline erase rates across rows whereas {\mu}_{t}{\bm{1}} enforces a uniform channel scalar, [Remark 3.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Remark 3.3 (Regularization of oblique erase variance by low-rank projections). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), which confirms that the dynamic action of the erase gate is captured mainly by {\mu}_{t} along the active key subspace.

##### Counterfactual Readout Gain.

[Table 22](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T22 "In I.3 Counterfactual Interventions and Memory-Horizon Diagnostics ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the full counterfactual readout sweep (Figure [12 a](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F12 "Figure 12 ‣ Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) on both 166\text{M} static-{\bm{\gamma}} checkpoints (Muir static {\bm{\gamma}} with gate ranks (64,64,128,256,0) and uniform Muir static {\bm{\gamma}} with gate ranks (128,128,128,128,0)). In each head, the surrogate profile is rescaled to match the \ell_{2} norm of the learned {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} (setting dead channels with {\rho}_{j}<10^{-10} to the within-head median live {\rho}_{j} prior to norm-matching) so that \Delta\text{NLL} isolates the effect of the relative channel-weighting shape.

Beyond the power-law family {\gamma}_{j}(p)\propto{\rho}_{j}^{-p} (p\in[0,1]), we evaluate three additional controls: (i) randomly permuting the learned {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} entries across channels within each head (which preserves the exact per-head gain distribution while breaking alignment with {\rho}_{j}^{-1}) degrades validation NLL by +0.130\pm 0.010\text{ nats} in Muir and +0.141\pm 0.011\text{ nats} in uniform Muir (worse than unit gain p=0), which shows that the benefit of {\bm{\gamma}} comes specifically from ordering gains inversely with channel row energy; (ii) inverting the power law to amplify slow rows ({\gamma}_{j}\propto{\rho}_{j}^{+1}) causes a loss spike of +2.291\pm 0.037\text{ nats} in Muir (+2.541\pm 0.038\text{ nats} in uniform Muir); and (iii) replacing {\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{stat}} with the unregularized single-horizon ratio g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})/{m}_{j}^{\ast} ([Table 22](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T22 "In I.3 Counterfactual Interventions and Memory-Horizon Diagnostics ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), right) achieves its lowest loss at intermediate horizons H\in[64,256] tokens (+0.768\text{ nats} at H=64), which confirms that language modeling queries integrate a broad mixture of target lags ([Remark 3.9](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem9 "Remark 3.9 (Suboptimality of full inverse-RMS whitening). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")).

Across all horizons H, however, the raw ratio g_{H}({\lambda}_{j})/{m}_{j}^{\ast} incurs a larger \Delta\text{NLL} than the fractional power law for two reasons. First, because post-\mathrm{SiLU} keys concentrate energy onto a few active channels (\eta_{\mathrm{eff}}({\bm{k}}_{t})\approx 6\text{--}7; [Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.T3 "In Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")), dividing directly by {m}_{j}^{\ast}\propto{c}_{j} assigns large weights {c}_{j}^{-1} to weakly active channels ({c}_{j}\ll 1/d_{k}), dominating the head norm budget and suppressing active channels. Second, on slow channels with 2\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}>H-1, the local exponent p(\tau_{\mathrm{eff},j}) in [Corollary 3.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem8 "Corollary 3.8 (Horizon-marginalized static gain and fractional power law). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") exceeds 1 (p\to 2), over-damping the slow rows that store long-range context.

##### State Spectral Effective Rank and Memory Horizon.

Finally, to test the prediction of [Proposition 3.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem10 "Proposition 3.10 (Erase-readout substitution). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") and [Remark 3.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S3.Thmtheorem11 "Remark 3.11 (Mechanism of memory-horizon extension). ‣ 3.1 Gate Sensitivity and Readout Dynamics ‣ 3 Channel-Wise Gated Delta Memory Dynamics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") that relaxing write-time erasure \bar{\mu} ([Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.T3 "In Stationary Row-Energy Scaling. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout")) preserves more historical associations in {\bm{S}}_{t}, we measure two complementary dimensions of stored memory across all 384 heads at 1.34\text{B}: _spatial subspace capacity_ (how many orthogonal key-value directions coexist in {\bm{S}}_{t} at timestep t) and _temporal lookback range_ (how far back in token lag the readout {\bm{o}}_{t} attributes its output).

First, we quantify the spatial subspace capacity via spectral effective rank \mathrm{erank}({\bm{S}}_{t})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\exp(-\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}{p}_{j}\log{p}_{j}), where {p}_{j}=\sigma_{j}^{2}({\bm{S}}_{t})/\|{\bm{S}}_{t}\|_{F}^{2} is the fraction of state Frobenius energy in the j-th singular direction of the single-head state {\bm{S}}_{t} (\mathrm{erank}({\bm{S}}_{t})=K when K orthogonal key-value pairs are stored with equal energy). Second, to quantify temporal lookback range, unrolling the linear recurrence {\bm{S}}_{t}={\bm{A}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t-1}+{\bm{k}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\bm{v}}_{t} with single-step transition {\bm{A}}_{s}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\mathbf{I}-{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\bm{k}}_{s})\Diag({\bm{\alpha}}_{s}) expresses the readout {\bm{o}}_{t}=\hat{\bm{q}}_{t}{\bm{S}}_{t} (\hat{\bm{q}}_{t}={\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t}) as a linear-attention sum over all past written values \hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell} (\ell\in\{0,\dotsc,t-1\}):

{\bm{o}}_{t}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}{a}_{t,t-\ell}\,\hat{\bm{v}}_{t-\ell},\qquad{a}_{t,t-\ell}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\hat{\bm{q}}_{t}\,{\bm{\Phi}}_{t,t-\ell}\,{\bm{k}}_{t-\ell}^{\top},\qquad{\bm{\Phi}}_{t,s}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\bm{A}}_{t}{\bm{A}}_{t-1}\cdots{\bm{A}}_{s+1},

with {\bm{\Phi}}_{t,t}=\mathbf{I}, where {a}_{t,t-\ell}\in\mathbb{R} is the effective attention weight from query timestep t to the token written \ell steps earlier. For late query positions t\geq 2{,}048 (where at least 2{,}048 steps of context are available), we define the 50\%-mass memory horizon L_{50} as the smallest lag L at which the cumulative attribution \sum_{\ell=0}^{L}|{a}_{t,t-\ell}| reaches 50\% of the total historical attribution \sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}|{a}_{t,t-\ell}|. Across held-out sequences, mean \mathrm{erank}({\bm{S}}_{t}) increases by 16.1\% (6.16\to 7.15) and layer-mean median L_{50} by 59.6\% (49.3\to 78.7 tokens).

Table 23: Write-order MV-NIAH and hop-resolved variable tracking recall at \bm{1.34\text{B}} parameters. All results are evaluated over N=500 examples per task at 1\text{K} (top) and 4\text{K} context lengths (bottom). MV-NIAH columns V_{1}\text{--}V_{4} order the four target values bound to the shared key from oldest write (V_{1}) to most recent write (V_{4}), and VT columns H_{1}\text{--}H_{5} order the five target variables from earliest root binding (H_{1},VAR_1 = V) to latest alias (H_{5},VAR_5 = VAR_4).

### I.4 Multi-Value Recall and Multi-Hop Variable Tracking

[Table 23](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A9.T23 "In State Spectral Effective Rank and Memory Horizon. ‣ I.3 Counterfactual Interventions and Memory-Horizon Diagnostics ‣ Appendix I Mechanistic Probe Protocols and Supplementary Diagnostics ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the complete write-order MV-NIAH (V_{1}\text{--}V_{4}) and hop-resolved VT (H_{1}\text{--}H_{5}) recall accuracies across the 1.34\text{B} models at 1\text{K} and 4\text{K} context lengths (N=500 prompts per task).

Using the 1\text{K} RULER VT protocol and per-target scoring from [Section H.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A8.SS2 "H.2 Associative Recall ‣ Appendix H Evaluation Protocols and Supplementary Results ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout"), we track how each written VT hop statement H_{m} at s_{m} (m\in\{1,\dotsc,5\}) is preserved in {\bm{S}}_{t} and read out at generation time across N=16 held-out prompts. At each sequence milestone t\in\{s_{m},\dotsc,s_{5},t_{\mathrm{q}}\}, we measure the remaining initial-query-projected signal of needle H_{m} in {\bm{S}}_{t} (summed over the token span {\mathcal{T}}_{m} of statement H_{m} ending at s_{m}) via

R_{m}(t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{m}}|\hat{\bm{q}}_{t_{\mathrm{q}}}\,{\bm{\Phi}}_{t,s}\,{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}|\,\|\hat{\bm{v}}_{s}\|_{2},

averaged across the top-quartile active retrieval heads (ranked by total needle signal \sum_{m=1}^{5}R_{m}(t_{\mathrm{q}}) at t_{\mathrm{q}}) in the middle associative-memory layers (layers 8\text{--}19 of our 1.34\text{B} model). Because per-head \mathrm{RMSNorm}({\bm{o}}_{t}) cancels uniform multiplicative scaling of \hat{\bm{q}}_{t_{\mathrm{q}}} and \hat{\bm{v}}_{s}, within each prompt and model we normalize the head-mean needle trace to its initial value at write milestone s_{m},\tilde{R}_{m}(t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R_{m}(t)/R_{m}(s_{m}) (\tilde{R}_{m}(s_{m})=100\%), and average \tilde{R}_{m}(t) across the 16 prompts to isolate retention across {\bm{\Phi}}_{t,s} from pre-\mathrm{RMSNorm} gate scale and random needle placement. Figure [13 c](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") plots prompt-mean \tilde{R}_{1}(t) across milestones s_{1}\text{--}s_{5} to t_{\mathrm{q}} (with interquartile bands across the 16 prompts), and Figure [13 d](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports prompt-mean \tilde{R}_{m}(t) across all five needles H_{1}\text{--}H_{5}. Across the four teacher-forced answer-step transitions A_{1}\text{--}A_{4} (t_{1}<t_{2}<t_{3}<t_{4}, as the model emits \texttt{VAR\_1}\to\dotsb\to\texttt{VAR\_4} to chain each hop to the next), Figure [13 e](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#S6.F13 "Figure 13 ‣ Counterfactual Readout and Erase Interventions. ‣ 6 Mechanistic Analysis ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") reports the share of readout weight over the haystack {\mathcal{H}} placed on each needle H_{m} (excluding the trailing question and answer suffix, averaged over the top-10 active heads per answer step across the 16 prompts):

\tilde{a}_{m}(A_{k})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{m}}|{a}_{t_{k},s}|\,\|\hat{\bm{v}}_{s}\|_{2}}{\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{H}}}|{a}_{t_{k},s}|\,\|\hat{\bm{v}}_{s}\|_{2}},\qquad k\in\{1,2,3,4\}.

## Appendix J Reference Implementation

In this appendix, we provide a JAX implementation of the Muir token mixer and the boundary-pivoted chunkwise forward and backward passes for wide-matrix-unit accelerators. On GPUs, because replacing {\bm{q}}_{t} with \hat{\bm{q}}_{t}={\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t} reduces the Muir recurrence to the chunkwise linear system of GDN-2, the mixer in [Figure 14](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#A10.F14 "In Appendix J Reference Implementation ‣ Muir: Linear Attention with Sensitivity-Allocated Gating and Matched-Filter Readout") also runs directly on existing Triton kernels for GDN-2 [[Yang and Zhang, 2024](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib123), [Hatamizadeh et al., 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2611.08376#bib.bib42)] without kernel changes by \ell_{2}-normalizing queries and keys to unit norm ({\bm{q}}_{t},{\bm{k}}_{t}) _before_ forming \hat{\bm{q}}_{t}={\bm{\gamma}}_{t}\odot{\bm{q}}_{t}, and passing (\hat{\bm{q}}_{t},{\bm{k}}_{t},{\bm{v}}_{t},\log{\bm{\alpha}}_{t},{\bm{b}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{t}) with in-kernel query-key \ell_{2} normalization disabled so \hat{\bm{q}}_{t} is not re-normalized.

Muir token mixer. Fused low-rank gate projection (r_{f},r_{b},r_{g},r_{w},r_{q})=(64,64,128,128,128), dynamic matched-filter readout {\bm{\gamma}}_{t}, pre-permutation fusion, and post-\mathrm{RMSNorm} output gating.

import functools,jax

from jax import numpy as jnp

#Assumes depthwise causal_conv1d(u,w),l2_norm(x),and rms_norm(x,scale).

def muir_mixer(x,params,num_heads=16,chunk_size=256):

"""Muir token mixer(x:(B,T,d)bf16/fp32;c_f,a_log,gamma_stat,w_rms fp32)."""

batch,seq_len,d_model=x.shape

d_head,num_chunks=d_model//num_heads,seq_len//chunk_size

u=causal_conv1d(jnp.dot(x,params[’W_qkv’]),params[’w_conv’])

q,k,v=jnp.split(jax.nn.silu(u),3,axis=-1)

h=jnp.dot(x,params[’W_down’])

h_fb=h[…,:128].reshape(batch,seq_len,2,64)

h_gwq=h[…,128:].reshape(batch,seq_len,3,128)

z_f,z_b=jnp.einsum(’btgr,grd->gbtd’,h_fb,params[’W_up_fb’])

z_b=z_b+params[’c_b’]

z_g,z_w,z_q=(jnp.einsum(’btgr,grd->gbtd’,h_gwq,params[’W_up_gwq’])

+params[’c_gwq’][:,None,None,:])

q,k,v,z_f,z_b,z_w,z_q=[

t.reshape(batch,seq_len,num_heads,d_head)

for t in(q,k,v,z_f,z_b,z_w,z_q)

]

q_norm,k_norm=l2_norm(q),l2_norm(k)

q_hat=params[’gamma_stat’]*(2.0*jax.nn.sigmoid(z_q))*q_norm

k_hat=2.0*jax.nn.sigmoid(z_b)*k_norm

v_hat=v*jax.nn.sigmoid(z_w)

chunk_shape=(batch,num_chunks,chunk_size,num_heads,d_head)

q_hat,k_norm,k_hat,v_hat,z_f=[

t.astype(x.dtype).reshape(chunk_shape).transpose(1,0,3,2,4)

for t in(q_hat,k_norm,k_hat,v_hat,z_f)

]

log_alpha=-jnp.exp(params[’a_log’])[:,None,None]*jax.nn.softplus(

z_f.astype(jnp.float32)+params[’c_f’].reshape(num_heads,1,d_head))

out=muir_chunkwise(q_hat,k_norm,k_hat,v_hat,log_alpha)

out=out.transpose(1,0,3,2,4).reshape(batch,seq_len,num_heads,d_head)

out=rms_norm(out,params[’w_rms’])*jax.nn.silu(

z_g.reshape(batch,seq_len,num_heads,d_head))

return jnp.dot(out.astype(x.dtype).reshape(batch,seq_len,d_model),params[’W_o’])

Top-level chunkwise recurrence and VMEM cross-chunk state scan. Computes cumulative log-decay {\bm{\Lambda}} on the MXU, invokes the boundary-pivoted solver, forms ({\bm{P}}_{\mathrm{read}},{\bm{P}}_{\mathrm{bias}}), and scans {\bm{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{v}} in float32 (delegating the Stage (iii) scan backward pass to JAX autodiff for brevity).

def _mxu(lhs,rhs,high_prec=False,out_dtype=jnp.float32,fp32=None):

"""Batched MXU matmul(1-pass bf16;3-pass bf16 if high_prec;full fp32 if both fp32)."""

if fp32 is None:

fp32=(lhs.dtype==jnp.float32)and(rhs.dtype==jnp.float32)

in_dtype=jnp.float32 if(fp32 or high_prec)else jnp.bfloat16

prec=(jax.lax.Precision.HIGHEST if fp32 else

jax.lax.Precision.HIGH if high_prec else jax.lax.Precision.DEFAULT)

return jnp.matmul(lhs.astype(in_dtype),rhs.astype(in_dtype),precision=prec,

preferred_element_type=jnp.float32).astype(out_dtype)

def _clamp_neg(x):

return jnp.where(x<=0.0,x,0.0)

def _leaf_blocks(q_b,k_b,kh_b,lam_b,orig_dtype):

"""Evaluates causal readout M_leaf and strict-tril L_leaf on diagonal leaves."""

tril=jnp.tri(q_b.shape[-2],q_b.shape[-2],dtype=bool)

d_raw=_clamp_neg(lam_b[…,:,None,:]-lam_b[…,None,:,:])

k_dec=k_b[…,None,:,:]*jnp.exp(jnp.where(tril[…,None],d_raw,0.0))

L_blk=jnp.tril(jnp.sum(kh_b[…,:,None,:]*k_dec,axis=-1),k=-1)

M_blk=jnp.tril(jnp.sum(q_b[…,:,None,:]*k_dec,axis=-1))

return M_blk.astype(orig_dtype),L_blk

def muir_chunkwise(q_hat,k,k_hat,v_hat,log_alpha,leaf_size=8,init_state=None):

"""Top-level chunkwise recurrence and VMEM cross-chunk state scan."""

_,batch,num_heads,chunk_size,d_k=q_hat.shape

d_v,orig_dtype=v_hat.shape[-1],v_hat.dtype

lam=_mxu(jnp.tril(jnp.ones((chunk_size,chunk_size),jnp.float32)),

log_alpha,high_prec=True,fp32=(orig_dtype==jnp.float32))

pi=jnp.exp(_clamp_neg(lam))

rhs=jnp.concatenate([v_hat,(k_hat*pi).astype(orig_dtype)],axis=-1)

M,Z=_build_and_solve(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,rhs,leaf_size,orig_dtype)

q_hat_pi,pi_end=q_hat*pi,pi[…,-1,:]

k_tilde=(k*jnp.exp(_clamp_neg(lam[…,-1:,:]-lam))).astype(orig_dtype)

prop=_mxu(jnp.concatenate([M,k_tilde.mT],axis=-2),Z)

MU,K_tilde_U=prop[…,:chunk_size,:d_v],prop[…,chunk_size:,:d_v]

MY,K_tilde_Y=prop[…,:chunk_size,d_v:],prop[…,chunk_size:,d_v:]

eye_k=jnp.eye(d_k,dtype=jnp.float32)

A_state=pi_end[…,None]*eye_k-K_tilde_Y

P_read=jnp.concatenate([q_hat_pi-MY,A_state],axis=-2)

P_bias=jnp.concatenate([MU,K_tilde_U],axis=-2)

if init_state is None:

init_state=jnp.zeros((batch,num_heads,d_k,d_v),jnp.float32)

def scan_step(state,xs):

packed=_mxu(xs[0],state,high_prec=True,fp32=(orig_dtype==jnp.float32))+xs[1]

return packed[…,chunk_size:,:],packed[…,:chunk_size,:].astype(orig_dtype)

_,out=jax.lax.scan(scan_step,init_state,(P_read,P_bias))

return out

Boundary-pivoted matrix construction and block-triangular inversion. Exact nilpotent Neumann leaf inversion (n_{0}=8) and r-level division-free boundary-pivoted merge tree (C=256).

def _neumann_inv(L,mxu_dtype):

"""Exact nilpotent Neumann inverse(I+L)^{-1}on leaf blocks."""

n0,eye=L.shape[-1],jnp.eye(L.shape[-1],dtype=jnp.float32)

T_inv,L_pow,step=eye-L,L,2

while step<n0:

L_pow=jnp.tril(_mxu(L_pow,L_pow.astype(mxu_dtype)),k=-step)

T_inv=jnp.tril(T_inv+_mxu(T_inv,L_pow.astype(mxu_dtype)),k=-1)+eye

step*=2

return T_inv

def _build_and_solve_impl(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,rhs,leaf_size,orig_dtype):

"""Constructs M and T^{-1}via boundary-pivoted merges and solves T Z=rhs."""

prefix,(chunk_size,d_k)=q_hat.shape[:-2],q_hat.shape[-2:]

leaf_shape=(*prefix,chunk_size//leaf_size,leaf_size,d_k)

q_b,k_b,kh_b,lam_b=[t.reshape(leaf_shape)for t in(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam)]

M_blk,L_blk=_leaf_blocks(q_b,k_b,kh_b,lam_b,orig_dtype)

T_inv_blk=_neumann_inv(L_blk,orig_dtype).astype(orig_dtype)

n=leaf_size

while n<chunk_size:

p_shape=(*prefix,chunk_size//(2*n),2*n,d_k)

q_p,k_p,kh_p,lam_p=[t.reshape(p_shape)for t in(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam)]

k_L,lam_L=k_p[…,:n,:],lam_p[…,:n,:]

q_R,kh_R,lam_R=q_p[…,n:,:],kh_p[…,n:,:],lam_p[…,n:,:]

lam_piv=lam_L[…,-1:,:]

pi_R=jnp.exp(_clamp_neg(lam_R-lam_piv))

pi_L=jnp.exp(_clamp_neg(lam_piv-lam_L))

lhs_R=jnp.concatenate([kh_R*pi_R,q_R*pi_R],axis=-2).astype(orig_dtype)

TM_cross=_mxu(lhs_R,(k_L*pi_L).astype(orig_dtype).mT,out_dtype=orig_dtype)

T_cross,M_cross=TM_cross[…,:n,:],TM_cross[…,n:,:]

T_inv_L,T_inv_R=T_inv_blk[…,0::2,:,:],T_inv_blk[…,1::2,:,:]

T_cross_L=_mxu(T_cross,T_inv_L,out_dtype=orig_dtype)

X_21=-_mxu(T_inv_R,T_cross_L,out_dtype=orig_dtype)

T_inv_blk=jnp.block([[T_inv_L,jnp.zeros_like(X_21)],[X_21,T_inv_R]])

M_L,M_R=M_blk[…,0::2,:,:],M_blk[…,1::2,:,:]

M_blk=jnp.block([[M_L,jnp.zeros_like(M_cross)],[M_cross,M_R]])

n*=2

M,T_inv=M_blk[…,0,:,:],T_inv_blk[…,0,:,:]

return M,_mxu(T_inv,rhs,out_dtype=orig_dtype),T_inv

Custom chunkwise backward pass. Differentiates {\bm{Z}}={\bm{T}}^{-1}[\hat{\bm{V}}\;\,\hat{\bm{K}}_{\odot\pi}] directly from the saved ({\bm{T}}^{-1},{\bm{Z}}) and recomputes ({\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{R}},{\bm{\pi}}_{\mathcal{L}}) in VMEM across the r merge levels.

@functools.partial(jax.custom_vjp,nondiff_argnums=(5,6))

def _build_and_solve(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,rhs,leaf_size,orig_dtype):

return _build_and_solve_impl(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,rhs,leaf_size,orig_dtype)[:2]

def _build_and_solve_fwd(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,rhs,leaf_size,orig_dtype):

M,Z,T_inv=_build_and_solve_impl(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,rhs,leaf_size,orig_dtype)

return(M,Z),(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,T_inv,Z)

def _build_and_solve_bwd(leaf_size,orig_dtype,res,cotangents):

"""Memory-efficient backward pass recomputing boundary-pivot decays on the fly."""

q_hat,k,k_hat,lam,T_inv,Z=res

dM,dZ=cotangents[0].astype(orig_dtype),cotangents[1]

prefix,(chunk_size,d_k)=q_hat.shape[:-2],q_hat.shape[-2:]

def diag_blocks(mat,blk):

nb=chunk_size//blk

d=jnp.diagonal(mat.reshape(*prefix,nb,blk,nb,blk),axis1=-4,axis2=-2)

return jnp.moveaxis(d,-1,-3)

drhs=_mxu(T_inv.mT,dZ)

dL=-jnp.tril(_mxu(drhs,Z.mT,high_prec=True),k=-1)

leaf_in=[t.reshape(*prefix,chunk_size//leaf_size,leaf_size,d_k)

for t in(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam)]

_,leaf_vjp=jax.vjp(lambda*xs:_leaf_blocks(*xs,orig_dtype),*leaf_in)

d_leaf=(diag_blocks(dM,leaf_size),diag_blocks(dL,leaf_size))

dq,dk,dkh,dlam=[g.reshape(q_hat.shape)for g in leaf_vjp(d_leaf)]

n=leaf_size

while n<chunk_size:

p_shape=(*prefix,chunk_size//(2*n),2*n,d_k)

q_p,k_p,kh_p,lam_p=[t.reshape(p_shape)for t in(q_hat,k,k_hat,lam)]

k_L,lam_L=k_p[…,:n,:],lam_p[…,:n,:]

q_R,kh_R,lam_R=q_p[…,n:,:],kh_p[…,n:,:],lam_p[…,n:,:]

lam_piv=lam_L[…,-1:,:]

pi_R=jnp.exp(_clamp_neg(lam_R-lam_piv))

pi_L=jnp.exp(_clamp_neg(lam_piv-lam_L))

dT_c=diag_blocks(dL,2*n)[…,n:,:n].astype(orig_dtype)

dTM=jnp.concatenate([dT_c,diag_blocks(dM,2*n)[…,n:,:n]],axis=-2)

lhs_R=jnp.concatenate([kh_R*pi_R,q_R*pi_R],axis=-2).astype(orig_dtype)

dkh_q_R=_mxu(dTM,(k_L*pi_L).astype(orig_dtype))

dkh_R_pi,dq_R_pi=dkh_q_R[…,:n,:],dkh_q_R[…,n:,:]

dk_L_pi=_mxu(dTM.mT,lhs_R)

flux_R=jnp.where(lam_R<=lam_piv,(dkh_R_pi*kh_R+dq_R_pi*q_R)*pi_R,0.0)

flux_L=jnp.where(lam_piv<=lam_L,(dk_L_pi*k_L)*pi_L,0.0)

dlam_L=(-flux_L).at[…,-1:,:].add(jnp.sum(flux_L-flux_R,axis=-2,keepdims=True))

zeros=jnp.zeros_like(k_L)

dq+=jnp.concatenate([zeros,dq_R_pi*pi_R],axis=-2).reshape(q_hat.shape)

dkh+=jnp.concatenate([zeros,dkh_R_pi*pi_R],axis=-2).reshape(k_hat.shape)

dk+=jnp.concatenate([dk_L_pi*pi_L,zeros],axis=-2).reshape(k.shape)

dlam+=jnp.concatenate([dlam_L,flux_R],axis=-2).reshape(lam.shape)

n*=2

return(dq.astype(q_hat.dtype),dk.astype(k.dtype),dkh.astype(k_hat.dtype),

dlam.astype(lam.dtype),drhs.astype(orig_dtype))

_build_and_solve.defvjp(_build_and_solve_fwd,_build_and_solve_bwd)
